M(\Psi,\Phi)\leq-\frac{d^{2}(\Psi,\Phi)}{8}
.
In particular,

\sup_{\Psi:d(\Psi,\Phi)\geq\varepsilon}M(\Psi,\Phi)\leq-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}\quad\text{for every }\varepsilon>0.

By some basic calculations and Lemma
G.2
(ii), we obtain

\displaystyle M(\Psi,\Phi)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\{0,1\}}m_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)dP_{\Phi}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}m_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)p_{\Phi}(x,y)d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\log\bigg(\frac{p_{\Psi}(x,y)+p_{\Phi}(x,y)}{2p_{\Phi}(x,y)}\bigg)p_{\Phi}(x,y)d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}2\Bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p_{\Psi}(x,y)+p_{\Phi}(x,y)}{2p_{\Phi}(x,y)}}-1\Bigg)p_{\Phi}(x,y)d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}2\int_{\{0,1\}}\sqrt{\frac{p_{\Psi}(x,y)+p_{\Phi}(x,y)}{2p_{\Phi}(x,y)}}p_{\Phi}(x,y)d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)-2

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p_{\Psi}(x,y)+p_{\Phi}(x,y)}{2}}-\sqrt{p_{\Phi}(x,y)}\bigg)^{2}d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

and by Lemma
G.1
,

\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{p_{\Psi}(x,y)+p_{\Phi}(x,y)}{2}}-\sqrt{p_{\Phi}(x,y)}\bigg)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{16}\big(\sqrt{p_{\Psi}(x,y)}-\sqrt{p_{\Phi}(x,y)}\big)^{2}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle M(\Psi,\Phi)\leq-\frac{1}{16}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\big(\sqrt{p_{\Psi}(x,y)}-\sqrt{p_{\Phi}(x,y)}\big)^{2}d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)=-\frac{1}{8}h^{2}(p_{\Psi},p_{\Phi})=-\frac{1}{8}d^{2}(\Psi,\Phi).

Now for any
\varepsilon>0
and every
\Psi\in\mathcal{F}
satisfying
d(\Psi,\Phi)\geq\varepsilon
, we have

M(\Psi,\Phi)\leq-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}
and the assertion follows.
∎

Note that the previous result implies
\Phi\in\operatorname*{argmax}_{\Psi\in\mathcal{F}}M(\Psi,\Phi)
.
Moreover, we obtain
M(\Psi,\Phi)=0
if and only if
\Psi=\Phi
almost surely.

As an intermediate step, before we prove that the difference between
M_{n}
and
M
converges uniformly in probability over
\mathcal{F}
, we derive an upper bound uniformly in
\Phi
on the bracketing numbers of the set of functions
m_{\Psi,\Phi}
.

Proposition A.3
.

Let
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{m_{\Psi,\Phi}\ |\ \Psi\in\mathcal{F}\}
. Then, there exists a constant
C>0
, such that for all

\delta>0
,

\sup_{\Phi\in\mathcal{F}}N_{[]}\big(\delta,\mathcal{G}_{\Phi},L^{1}(P_{\Phi})\big)\leq N_{[]}(\delta/2,\mathcal{F},L^{1}(P_{X}))\leq C^{1/\delta}.

The second inequality is an immediate consequence of Theorem 2.7.9 in
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
,
where the constructed brackets in particular belong to
\mathcal{F}
.
For arbitrary
\Psi\in\mathcal{F}
, let

[\Psi_{L},\Psi^{U}]
denote a corresponding
\delta
-bracket for
\Psi
, where
\Psi_{L},\Psi^{U}\in\mathcal{F}
. Let

\displaystyle p_{L}\colon\mathbb{R}\times\{0,1\}\to\bar{\mathbb{R}},

\displaystyle\quad p_{L}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Psi_{L}(x)^{y}(1-\Psi^{U}(x))^{1-y},

\displaystyle p^{U}\colon\mathbb{R}\times\{0,1\}\to\bar{\mathbb{R}},

\displaystyle\quad p^{U}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Psi^{U}(x)^{y}(1-\Psi_{L}(x))^{1-y}

and define

\displaystyle f_{\Phi,L}\colon\mathbb{R}\times\{0,1\}\to\bar{\mathbb{R}},

\displaystyle\quad f_{\Phi,L}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{p_{L}(x,y)+p_{\Phi}(x,y)}{2p_{\Phi}(x,y)},

\displaystyle f_{\Phi}^{U}\colon\mathbb{R}\times\{0,1\}\to\bar{\mathbb{R}},

\displaystyle\quad f_{\Phi}^{U}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{p^{U}(x,y)+p_{\Phi}(x,y)}{2p_{\Phi}(x,y)}.

Then, for every
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle f_{\Phi,L}(x,0)=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1-\Psi^{U}(x)}{2(1-\Phi(x))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1-\Psi(x)}{2(1-\Phi(x))}=f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,0),

\displaystyle f_{\Phi,L}(x,1)=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\Psi_{L}(x)}{2\Phi(x)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\Psi(x)}{2\Phi(x)}=f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,1),

\displaystyle f_{\Phi}^{U}(x,0)=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1-\Psi_{L}(x)}{2(1-\Phi(x))}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1-\Psi(x)}{2(1-\Phi(x))}=f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,0),

\displaystyle f_{n}^{U}(x,1)=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\Phi^{U}(x)}{2\Phi(x)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\Psi(x)}{2\Phi(x)}=f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,1),

i.e. for every
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}\times\{0,1\}
, we have

f_{\Phi,L}(x,y)\leq f_{\Phi,\Psi}(x,y)\leq f_{\Phi}^{U}(x,y).

Defining

\displaystyle m_{\Phi,L}\colon\mathbb{R}\times\{0,1\}\to\bar{\mathbb{R}},

\displaystyle\quad m_{\Phi,L}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\log(f_{\Phi,L}(x,y)),

\displaystyle m_{\Phi}^{U}\colon\mathbb{R}\times\{0,1\}\to\bar{\mathbb{R}},

\displaystyle\quad m_{\Phi}^{U}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\log(f_{\Phi}^{U}(x,y)),

we have

m_{\Phi,L}(x,y)\leq m_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)\leq m_{\Phi}^{U}(x,y)

by definition of
m_{\Psi,\Phi}
. Moreover, from Lemma
G.2
(i), we obtain

\displaystyle\|m_{\Phi}^{U}-m_{\Phi,L}\|_{1,P_{\Phi}}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\log\Big(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{p^{U}}{2p_{\Phi}}\Big)-\log\Big(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{p_{L}}{2p_{\Phi}}\Big)\Big\|_{1,P_{\Phi}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Big\|\frac{p^{U}}{2p_{\Phi}}-\frac{p_{L}}{2p_{\Phi}}\Big\|_{1,P_{\Phi}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\Big|p^{U}(x,y)-p_{L}(x,y)\Big|\mathds{1}_{\{p_{\Phi}(x,y)>0\}}d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\Phi^{U}(x)-\Phi_{L}(x)|+|1-\Phi^{U}(x)-(1-\Phi_{L}(x))|dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle=2\|\Phi^{U}-\Phi_{L}\|_{1,P_{X}}\leq 2\delta.

Thus,
[m_{\Phi,L},m_{\Phi}^{U}]
is a
2\delta
-bracket enclosing
m_{\Psi,\Phi}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
, where both,
m_{\Phi,L}

and
m_{\Phi}^{U}
, are contained in
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
by construction. Consequently,

N_{[]}\big(\delta,\mathcal{G}_{\Phi},L^{1}(P_{\Phi})\big)\leq N_{[]}(\delta/2,\mathcal{F},L^{1}(P_{X})).

∎

Uniformly in
\Phi
, the next Lemma states uniform convergence in probability of the difference

M_{n}(\cdot,\Phi)-M(\cdot,\Phi)
over
\mathcal{F}
, which will later
allow us to derive convergence of the approximate maximizers of
M_{n}(\cdot,\Phi)
and
M(\cdot,\Phi)
. The proof makes
use of
Proposition
A.3
and is based on a typical Glivenko-Cantelli argument (cf. Lemma 3.1 in
van de Geer (2010)
), which we had to modify for our setting to take into account the
\Phi
-dependent function
classes.

Lemma A.4
.

For every
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\sup_{\Phi\in\mathcal{F}}P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\bigg(\sup_{\Psi\in\mathcal{F}}|M_{n}(\Psi,\Phi)-M(\Psi,\Phi)|>\varepsilon\bigg)\longrightarrow 0\quad\text{as }n\longrightarrow\infty.

First of all, note that for
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
defined as in Proposition
A.3
, we have

\sup_{\Psi\in\mathcal{F}}|M_{n}(\Psi,\Phi)-M(\Psi,\Phi)|=\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}[g(X,Y)]\bigg|.

From Lemma
A.3
, we know that there exists
C>0
, independent of
\Phi
, such that

N_{[]}\big(\delta,\mathcal{G}_{\Phi},L^{1}(P_{\Phi})\big)\leq C^{1/\delta}\qquad\text{for all }\delta>0\text{ and all }\Phi\in\mathcal{F}.

Thus, for every
\delta>0
, there exists a
\delta
-bracketing set

\{[g_{j,L}^{\Phi},g_{j}^{U,\Phi}]\}_{j=1,\dots,N(\delta)}
for
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
with respect to
P_{\Phi}
, satisfying

N(\delta)\leq C^{1/\delta}
and
g_{j,L}^{\Phi},g_{j}^{U,\Phi}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
for
j=1,\dots,N(\delta)
, for every

\Phi\in\mathcal{F}
. More specifically, this means

\Big\|g_{j}^{U,\Phi}-g_{j,L}^{\Phi}\Big\|_{1,P_{\Phi}}\leq\delta

for
j=1,\dots,N(\delta)
and that for every
g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
, there exists
j\in\{1,\dots,N(\delta)\}
, such that

g_{j,L}^{\Phi}\leq g\leq g_{j}^{U,\Phi}.

Thus, for every
g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}[g(X,Y)]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{j}^{U,\Phi}(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}\Big[g_{j}^{U,\Phi}(X,Y)\Big]+\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}\Big[g_{j}^{U,\Phi}(X,Y)\Big]-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}[g(X,Y)]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{j}^{U,\Phi}(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}\Big[g_{j}^{U,\Phi}(X,Y)\Big]+\Big\|g_{j}^{U,\Phi}-g\Big\|_{1,P_{\Phi}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{j}^{U,\Phi}(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}\Big[g_{j}^{U,\Phi}(X,Y)\Big]+\delta.

Similarly, we obtain

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}[g(X,Y)]\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{j,L}^{\Phi}(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}\Big[g_{j,L}^{\Phi}(X,Y)\Big]-\delta,

implying

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}[g(X,Y)]\bigg|\leq\max\bigg\{\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{j}^{U,\Phi}(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}\Big[g_{j}^{U,\Phi}(X,Y)\Big]\bigg|,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{j,L}^{\Phi}(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}\Big[g_{j,L}^{\Phi}(X,Y)\Big]\bigg|\bigg\}+\delta.

Defining

\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,\delta}^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\big\{g_{j,L}^{\Phi}\ |\ j=1,\dots,N(\delta)\big\}\cup\big\{g_{j}^{U,\Phi}\ |\ j=1,\dots,N(\delta)\big\}
,
we know from Proposition
A.3
, that
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,\delta}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
and obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}[g(X,Y)]\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\max_{j=1,\dots,N(\delta)}\max\bigg\{\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{j}^{U,\Phi}(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}\Big[g_{j}^{U,\Phi}(X,Y)\Big]\bigg|,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{j,L}^{\Phi}(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}\Big[g_{j,L}^{\Phi}(X,Y)\Big]\bigg|\bigg\}+\delta

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,\delta}^{\prime}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}[g(X,Y)]\bigg|+\delta.

Now for every
\varepsilon>0
and
\delta=\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
, we have
N_{n}\leq C^{1/\delta}=C^{2/\varepsilon}

and we obtain from an application of Chebyshev’s inequality, that

\displaystyle P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\bigg(\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,\delta}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}[g(X,Y)]\bigg|\geq\varepsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\bigg(\max_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,\delta}^{\prime}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}[g(X,Y)]\bigg|+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\geq\varepsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,\delta}^{\prime}}P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\bigg(\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(x_{i},y_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}[g(X,Y)]\bigg|\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq\sum_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,\delta}^{\prime}}\frac{4}{\varepsilon^{2}}\frac{\operatorname{Var}_{\Phi}(g(X,Y))}{n}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq C^{2/\varepsilon}\frac{4}{\varepsilon^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,\delta}}\operatorname{Var}_{\Phi}(g(X,Y)).

Assuming the variance is uniformly bounded in
\Phi
, the assertion follows immediately. To this aim, note first that for
arbitrary
\Psi\in\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}_{\Phi}(m_{\Psi,\Phi}(X,Y))

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\log(f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y))^{2}p_{\Phi}(x,y)d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\log(f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y))^{2}p_{\Phi}(x,y)\mathds{1}_{\{f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)\geq 1\}}d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\log(f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y))^{2}p_{\Phi}(x,y)\mathds{1}_{\{f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)<1\}}d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x).

By applying Lemma
G.2
(ii), as well as using the fact that
0\leq p_{\Psi}\leq 1
for every

\Psi\in\mathcal{F}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\log(f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y))^{2}p_{\Phi}(x,y)\mathds{1}_{\{f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)\geq 1\}}d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 4\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\Big(\sqrt{f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)}-1\Big)^{2}p_{\Phi}(x,y)\mathds{1}_{\{f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)\geq 1\}}d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 4\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\Big(f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)-2\sqrt{f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)}+1\Big)p_{\Phi}(x,y)d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 4\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\Big(\frac{p_{\Psi}(x,y)+p_{\Phi}(x,y)}{2}+p_{\Phi}(x,y)\Big)d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 4\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}2d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x).

Similarly, by an application of Lemma
G.2
(iii) and by using
p_{\Psi}(x,y)+p_{\Phi}(x,y)\geq p_{\Phi}(x,y)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\log(f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y))^{2}p_{\Phi}(x,y)\mathds{1}_{\{f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)<1\}}d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\bigg(1-\frac{1}{f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)}\bigg)^{2}p_{\Phi}(x,y)\mathds{1}_{\{f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)<1\}}d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\bigg(1-\frac{2}{f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)}+\frac{1}{f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)^{2}}\bigg)p_{\Phi}(x,y)d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\bigg(1+\frac{1}{f_{\Psi,\Phi}(x,y)^{2}}\bigg)p_{\Phi}(x,y)d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}\bigg(1+4\frac{p_{\Phi}(x,y)^{2}}{p_{\Phi}(x,y)^{2}}\bigg)p_{\Phi}(x,y)d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad=5\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\{0,1\}}p_{\Phi}(x,y)d\zeta(y)dP_{X}(x).

Combining these results, we have shown that
\operatorname{Var}_{\Phi}(m_{\Psi,\Phi}(X,Y))\leq 21
.
∎

Based on Lemmas
A.1
,
A.2
and
A.4
, we can now prove
Proposition
2.3
, following the proof of Theorem 5.7 in
van der Vaart (1998)
.

For every
\varepsilon>0
, Lemma
A.2
shows that

M(\Psi,\Phi)\leq-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}
for all
\Psi\in\mathcal{F}
with
d(\Psi,\Phi)\geq\varepsilon
. Thus,

\big\{d(\hat{\Phi}_{n},\Phi)\geq\varepsilon\big\}\subset\bigg\{M(\hat{\Phi}_{n},\Phi)\leq-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}\bigg\}=\bigg\{-M(\hat{\Phi}_{n},\Phi)\geq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}\bigg\}\quad P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}-\text{a.s.}

From Lemma
A.1
, we obtain

-M(\hat{\Phi}_{n},\Phi)\leq M_{n}(\hat{\Phi}_{n},\Phi)-M(\hat{\Phi}_{n},\Phi)\leq\sup_{\Psi\in\mathcal{F}}|M_{n}(\Psi,\Phi)-M(\Psi,\Phi)|\quad P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}-\text{a.s.}

Consequently,

\bigg\{-M(\hat{\Phi}_{n},\Phi)\geq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}\bigg\}\subset\bigg\{\sup_{\Psi\in\mathcal{F}}|M_{n}(\Psi,\Phi)-M(\Psi,\Phi)|\geq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}\bigg\}\quad P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}-\text{a.s.}

and by Lemma
A.4
, we have for all
\varepsilon>0
,

\sup_{\Phi\in\mathcal{F}}P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\big(d(\hat{\Phi}_{n},\Phi)\geq\varepsilon\big)\leq\sup_{\Phi\in\mathcal{F}}P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\bigg(\sup_{\Psi\in\mathcal{F}}|M_{n}(\Psi,\Phi)-M(\Psi,\Phi)|\geq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{8}\bigg)\longrightarrow 0\quad\text{as }n\longrightarrow\infty.

∎

A.3
Proof of Corollary
2.4

The idea of the proof is to show for every subsequence of
D_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\hat{\Phi}_{n}-\Phi_{n}
that
there exists a subsubsequence converging uniformly to
0
in probability under
\mathbb{P}
.
To make this precise, we start with an arbitrary subsequence of
(D_{n})
, which we will denote by
(D_{n})
again for ease
of notation. Then, by (
2.5
) and the characterization of convergence in probability in terms of almost surely convergent
subsequences, there exists a subsubsequence
(n_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j}}(x)-\Phi_{n_{j}}(x)|dP_{X}(x)\longrightarrow 0\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}\quad\text{as }j\longrightarrow\infty.

Define

S_{\mathbb{P}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigg\{\omega\in\Omega\ \bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j}}(\omega,x)-\Phi_{n_{j}}(x)|dP_{X}(x)\longrightarrow 0\text{ as }j\longrightarrow\infty\bigg\}

and consider for fixed
\omega\in S_{\mathbb{P}}
an arbitrary subsequence of
D_{n_{j}}(\omega,\cdot)
, which we denote by

D_{n_{j}}(\omega,\cdot)
again. Then, by an application of Markov’s inequality with respect to
P_{X}
on
\mathbb{R}
, we obtain
for every
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle P_{X}(|D_{n_{j}}(\omega,\cdot)|>\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{X}\big[|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j}}(\omega,\cdot)-\Phi_{n_{j}}(\cdot)|\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j}}(\omega,x)-\Phi_{n_{j}}(x)|dP_{X}(x)\longrightarrow 0\quad\text{as }j\longrightarrow\infty,

by definition of
S_{\mathbb{P}}
. In different notation, this means

|D_{n_{j}}(\omega,\cdot)|=|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j}}(\omega,\cdot)-\Phi_{n_{j}}(\cdot)|\longrightarrow_{P_{X}}0\quad\text{as }j\longrightarrow\infty.

But then, again, there exists another increasing sequence
(j_{k}^{\omega})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
,
depending on
\omega
, satisfying
j_{k}^{\omega}\longrightarrow\infty
for
k\longrightarrow\infty
, such that

|D_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,\cdot)|=|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,\cdot)-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\cdot)|\longrightarrow 0\quad P_{X}\text{-a.s.}\quad\text{as }k\longrightarrow\infty.

Now, similar as before, we define

S_{P_{X}}(\omega)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\big\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{o}\mid|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,\cdot)-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\cdot)|\longrightarrow 0\text{ as }k\longrightarrow\infty\big\},

where
\mathcal{X}^{o}
denotes the interior of
\mathcal{X}
. Then, for arbitrary but fixed
x_{0}\in\mathcal{X}^{o}\setminus S_{P_{X}}(\omega)

and for all
\varepsilon>0
, the fact that
P_{X}
has a
Lebesgue density being positive on
\mathcal{X}^{o}
implies the existence of
x_{1},x_{2}\in S_{P_{X}}(\omega)
with

x_{1}<x_{0}<x_{2}
. Moreover, from Lemma
G.5
, we know that there exists
K\in\mathbb{N}
, such
that

|\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{2})-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{1})|<\varepsilon/5

for every
k>K
. By choosing
K\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large, we also have

|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x_{1})-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{1})|<\varepsilon/5\quad\text{ and }\quad|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x_{2})-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{2})|<\varepsilon/5

for all
k>K
, and obtain that
|D_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x_{0})|
is bounded by

\displaystyle|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x_{0})-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x_{0})-\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x_{1})|+|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x_{1})-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{1})|+|\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{1})-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x_{2})-\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x_{1})|+|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x_{1})-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{1})|+|\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{1})-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{2})|

\displaystyle<|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x_{2})-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{2})|+|\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{2})-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{1})|+|\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x_{1})-\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x_{1})|+\frac{2\varepsilon}{5},

which is bounded by
\varepsilon
and
where we used the fact that both
\hat{\Phi}_{n}
and
\Phi_{n}
are increasing in
x
. Thus, we have shown

|D_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x)|=|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x)-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x)|\longrightarrow 0\quad\text{as }k\longrightarrow\infty

not only for
x\in S_{P_{X}}(\omega)
, but for all
x\in\mathcal{X}^{o}
.
Utilizing that pointwise convergent
[0,1]
-valued isotonic functions with continuous limit also converge uniformly on
compacts, we obtain for any compact interval
I\subset\mathcal{X}^{o}
,

\sup_{x\in I}|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(\omega,x)-\Phi_{n_{j_{k}^{\omega}}}(x)|\longrightarrow 0\quad\text{as }k\longrightarrow\infty.

But this means, that for any arbitrary subsequence of
D_{n_{j}}(\omega,\cdot)
, we found a subsubsequence converging to
zero uniformly on
I
, implying by the subsequence argument,

\sup_{x\in I}|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j}}(\omega,x)-\Phi_{n_{j}}(x)|\longrightarrow 0\quad\text{as }j\longrightarrow\infty.

But because
\omega\in S_{\mathbb{P}}
was arbitrary, we have actually shown by definition of
S_{\mathbb{P}}
, that

\sup_{x\in I}|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j}}(\cdot,x)-\Phi_{n_{j}}(x)|\longrightarrow 0\quad\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}\quad\text{as }j\longrightarrow\infty,

implying

\sup_{x\in I}|\hat{\Phi}_{n_{j}}(\cdot,x)-\Phi_{n_{j}}(x)|\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0\quad\text{as }j\longrightarrow\infty.

Applying the subsequence argument again, we conclude

\sup_{x\in I}|\hat{\Phi}_{n}(\cdot,x)-\Phi_{n}(x)|\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0\quad\text{as }n\longrightarrow\infty.

∎

B
Remaining proofs of Section
3

In this section, we prove Theorem
3.1
, Theorem
3.2
and the auxiliary results used in the proof of Theorem
3.2
.

B.1
Proof of Theorem
3.1

Assume there exist
\Phi_{0,n},\Phi_{1,n}\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}
with

|\Phi_{0,n}(x_{0})-\Phi_{1,n}(x_{0})|\geq 2C\max\Big\{n^{-1/2},\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-1/3}\Big\}

(B.1)

for some
C>0
. Provided that

\displaystyle h^{2}\big(P_{0,n}^{\otimes n},P_{1,n}^{\otimes n}\big)\leq\alpha<2

(B.2)

with
P_{0,n}^{\otimes n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=P_{\Phi_{0,n}}^{\otimes n}
and

P_{1,n}^{\otimes n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=P_{\Phi_{1,n}}^{\otimes n}
,
the general reduction scheme of Chapter 2.2 in
Tsybakov (2009)
and Theorem 2.2 (ii) in
Tsybakov (2009)
then reveal

\inf_{T_{n}^{\delta}(x_{0})}\sup_{\Phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}}P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\Big(\Big(\sqrt{n}\wedge\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{1/3}\Big)\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x_{0})-\Phi(x_{0})\big|\geq C\Big)\geq\frac{1}{2}\big(1-\sqrt{\alpha(1-\alpha)/4}\big)>0.

In what follows, we construct
\Phi_{0,n}
and
\Phi_{1,n}
with properties (
B.1
) and (
B.2
) for

\delta\geq n^{-1/2}
and
\delta<n^{-1/2}
separately, noting that

\max\{n^{-1/2},(\frac{n}{\delta})^{-1/3}\}=(\frac{n}{\delta})^{-1/3}
if and
only if
\delta\geq n^{-1/2}
. In both cases, the constructed hypotheses will satisfy
\Phi_{0,n}\geq\Phi_{1,n}
(hence
1-\Phi_{0,n}\leq 1-\Phi_{1,n}
). Thus,

\displaystyle h^{2}\big(P_{0,n}^{\otimes n},P_{1,n}^{\otimes n}\big)\leq nh^{2}\big(P_{0,n},P_{1,n}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{n}{2}\int_{-T}^{T}\Big(\sqrt{\Phi_{0,n}(x)}-\sqrt{\Phi_{1,n}(x)}\Big)^{2}+\Big(\sqrt{1-\Phi_{0,n}(x)}-\sqrt{1-\Phi_{1,n}(x)}\Big)^{2}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{n}{2}\int_{-T}^{T}\bigg(\frac{\Phi_{0,n}(x)-\Phi_{1,n}(x)}{\sqrt{\Phi_{0,n}(x)}+\sqrt{\Phi_{1,n}(x)}}\bigg)^{2}+\bigg(\frac{\Phi_{0,n}(x)-\Phi_{1,n}(x)}{\sqrt{1-\Phi_{0,n}(x)}+\sqrt{1-\Phi_{1,n}(x)}}\bigg)^{2}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{n}{8}\int_{-T}^{T}(\Phi_{0,n}(x)-\Phi_{1,n}(x))^{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{\Phi_{1,n}(x)}+\frac{1}{1-\Phi_{0,n}(x)}\bigg)dP_{X}(x).

\bullet
We start with the case
\delta<n^{-1/2}
. Let
0<C<1/\sqrt{2}
,
\eta_{n,\delta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=1/2-\delta T-Cn^{-1/2}
and define

\displaystyle\Phi_{0,n}\colon\mathbb{R}\to[0,1],

\displaystyle\quad\Phi_{0,n}|_{[-T,T]}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\delta(x+T)+\eta_{n,\delta}+2Cn^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\Phi_{1,n}\colon\mathbb{R}\to[0,1],

\displaystyle\quad\Phi_{1,n}|_{[-T,T]}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\delta(x+T)+\eta_{n,\delta},

where both functions are defined outside
[-T,T]
by their values at the respective boundaries. Obviously,

\Phi_{0,n},\Phi_{1,n}\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}
and

|\Phi_{0,n}(x_{0})-\Phi_{1,n}(x_{0})|=2Cn^{-1/2}.

Next, for
n\geq 16(C+T)^{2}
and all
x\in[-T,T]
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{1,n}(x)

\displaystyle\geq\Phi_{1,n}(-T)=\eta_{n,\delta}\geq 1/2-n^{-1/2}(C+T)\geq 1/4,

\displaystyle 1-\Phi_{0,n}(x)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\Phi_{0,n}(T)=1-2T\delta-\eta_{n,\delta}-2Cn^{-1/2}=\eta_{n,\delta}\geq 1/4.

Consequently, for
\alpha=4C^{2}
,

h^{2}\big(P_{0,n}^{\otimes n},P_{1,n}^{\otimes n}\big)\leq\frac{n}{8}\int_{-T}^{T}8(\Phi_{0,n}(x)-\Phi_{1,n}(x))^{2}dP_{X}(x)=4C^{2}=\alpha<2.

Thus, (
B.1
) and (
B.2
) are satisfied for all
\delta\in[0,n^{-1/2})
and
n\geq 16(C+T)^{2}
, whence

\inf_{T_{n}^{\delta}(x_{0})}\sup_{\Phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}}P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\Big(\Big(\sqrt{n}\wedge\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{1/3}\Big)\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x_{0})-\Phi(x_{0})\big|\geq C\Big)>\frac{1}{2}\big(1-\sqrt{\alpha(1-\alpha)/4}\big)>0.

\bullet
For
\delta\geq n^{-1/2}
, assume
0<C<\min\{(4T)^{1/3}/8,(32\|p_{X}\|_{\infty})^{-1/3}\}
, define
\eta_{\delta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=1/2-\frac{\delta}{2}(x_{0}+T)
and
set

\displaystyle\Phi_{0,n}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\eta_{\delta}

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{2}(x+T)\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[-T,x_{0}-4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3})\}}

\displaystyle+\delta\Big(x+\frac{T-x_{0}+4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}}{2}\Big)\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[x_{0}-4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3},x_{0})\}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{2}\big(x+T+4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}\big)\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[x_{0},T]\}},

as well as

\displaystyle\Phi_{1,n}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\eta_{\delta}

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{2}(x+T)\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[-T,x_{0})\}}

\displaystyle+\delta\Big(x+\frac{T-x_{0}}{2}\Big)\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[x_{0},x_{0}+4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3})\}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\delta}{2}\big(x+T+4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}\big)\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[x_{0}+4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3},T]\}},

where both functions are defined outside
[-T,T]
by their values at the respective boundaries.

-T
x_{0}
T
1/4
3/4
\Phi_{0,n}
\Phi_{1,n}

Figure 7:
Visualization of
\Phi_{0,n}
and
\Phi_{1,n}
in case
\delta\geq n^{-1/2}
. Note that
|\Phi_{0,n}(x_{0})-\Phi_{1,n}(x_{0})|=2C(n/\delta)^{-1/3}
.

Obviously,
\Phi_{0,n},\Phi_{1,n}\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}
. A visualization of the hypotheses is given in Figure
7
. Note that

|\Phi_{0,n}(x_{0})-\Phi_{1,n}(x_{0})|=2C\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-1/3}.

Note further that for
n\geq 16^{3}C^{3}
and all
x\in[x_{0}-4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3},x_{0}+4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}]
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{1,n}(x)

\displaystyle\geq\Phi_{1,n}(x_{0}-4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3})=\frac{1}{2}-4C\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-1/3}\geq\frac{1}{4},

\displaystyle 1-\Phi_{0,n}(x)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\Phi_{0,n}(x_{0}+4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3})=\frac{1}{2}-4C\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-1/3}\geq\frac{1}{4}.

Thus, for
\alpha=8^{2}C^{3}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}
,

\displaystyle h^{2}\big(P_{0,n}^{\otimes n},P_{1,n}^{\otimes n}\big)

\displaystyle\leq n\int_{x_{0}-4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}}^{x_{0}+4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}}(\Phi_{0,n}(x)-\Phi_{1,n}(x))^{2}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle=n\int_{x_{0}-4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}}^{x_{0}}\Big(\frac{\delta}{2}(x-x_{0})+2C\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-1/3}\Big)^{2}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+n\int_{x_{0}}^{x_{0}+4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}}\Big(\frac{\delta}{2}(x_{0}-x)+2C\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-1/3}\Big)^{2}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\leq n\int_{x_{0}-4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}}^{x_{0}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{4}(x-x_{0})^{2}+4C^{2}\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-2/3}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+n\int_{x_{0}}^{x_{0}+4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}}\frac{\delta^{2}}{4}(x-x_{0})^{2}+4C^{2}\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-2/3}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\leq n\int_{x_{0}-4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}}^{x_{0}}8C^{2}\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-2/3}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+n\int_{x_{0}}^{x_{0}+4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}}8C^{2}\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-2/3}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\leq 8^{2}C^{3}n(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3}\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-2/3}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}=8^{2}C^{3}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}=\alpha<2

and we have

\inf_{T_{n}^{\delta}(x_{0})}\sup_{\Phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}}P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\Big(\Big(\sqrt{n}\wedge\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{1/3}\Big)\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x_{0})-\Phi(x_{0})\big|\geq C\Big)>\frac{1}{2}\big(1-\sqrt{\alpha(1-\alpha)/4}\big)>0

for all
\delta\in[n^{-1/2},1]
and all
n\geq 12^{3}C^{3}
.

In Summary,

\inf_{T_{n}^{\delta}(x_{0})}\sup_{\Phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}}P_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\Big(\Big(\sqrt{n}\wedge\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{1/3}\Big)\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x_{0})-\Phi(x_{0})\big|\geq C\Big)\geq\frac{1}{2}\big(1-\sqrt{\alpha(1-\alpha)/4}\big)>0

for
\alpha=\max\{4C^{2},8^{2}C^{3}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}\}
, all
\delta\in[0,1]
and

n>\max\{12^{3}C^{3},16(C+T)^{2}\}
and so the assertion follows. ∎

B.2
Proof of Theorem
3.2

Note first that for every
v\in\mathbb{R}
and any sequence
(r_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of real numbers, the switch
relation (Lemma
2.2
) reveals

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(r_{n}\big(\hat{\Phi}_{n}

\displaystyle(x_{0})-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})\big)\leq v\big)=\mathbb{P}\big(\hat{\Phi}_{n}(x_{0})\leq\Phi_{n}(x_{0})+r_{n}^{-1}v\big)

(B.3)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\operatorname*{argmin^{+}}_{s\in[-T,T]}\bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(Y_{i}^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})\big)\mathds{1}_{\{X_{i}\leq s\}}-r_{n}^{-1}\frac{v}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}_{\{X_{i}\leq s\}}\bigg\}\geq F_{n}^{-1}(F_{n}(x_{0}))\bigg).

\bullet
For the proof of (ii) and (iii), let
r_{n}=\sqrt{n}
and define

h_{n}\colon[-T,T]\times\{0,1\}\times[-T,T]\to\mathbb{R},\quad h_{n}(x,y,t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq t\}},

as well as
H_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{E}[h_{n}(X,Y^{n},t)]
. Note that multiplying a function inside the
\operatorname*{argmin^{+}}
by
\sqrt{n}

does not change the location of its minimum. Hence, by (
B.3
) and by utilizing that
F_{X}
is a strictly
isotonic bijection between
[-T,T]
and
[0,1]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\Phi}_{n}(x_{0})-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})\big)\leq v\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\operatorname*{argmin^{+}}_{s\in[-T,T]}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},s)-H_{n}(s)\big)+\sqrt{n}H_{n}(s)-\frac{v}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}_{\{X_{i}\leq s\}}\bigg\}\geq F_{n}^{-1}(F_{n}(x_{0}))\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\operatorname*{argmin^{+}}_{s\in[0,1]}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},F_{X}^{-1}(s))-H_{n}(F_{X}^{-1}(s))\big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sqrt{n}H_{n}(F_{X}^{-1}(s))-v\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}_{\{X_{i}\leq F_{X}^{-1}(s)\}}\bigg\}\geq F_{X}(F_{n}^{-1}(F_{n}(x_{0})))\bigg).

By Lemma
B.4
, the sequence inside the
\operatorname*{argmin}

converges weakly in
\ell^{\infty}([0,1])
to

\big(\sigma_{\Phi_{0}}W(s)+\sqrt{c}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(0)\mathbb{E}\big[(X-x_{0})^{\beta}\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(s)\}}\big]-vs\big)_{s\in[0,1]}

(B.4)

as long as
n\delta_{n}^{2\beta}\longrightarrow c\in[0,\infty)
and so Proposition
B.5

yields convergence in distribution of the respective
\operatorname*{argmin}
’s. By
Stryhn (1996)
for
c=0
and more general,
by an application of an obvious adjustment of Lemma A.2 in
Cattaneo et al. (2024)
to processes defined on a compact
interval (see
Cattaneo et al. (2025)
as well), we obtain under the condition in (ii) for any
c\geq 0

that the
\operatorname*{argmin}
of the process in (
B.4
) has a continuous distribution function. In particular,
F_{X}(x_{0})

is a continuity point of this distribution and
F_{X}(F_{n}^{-1}(F_{n}(x_{0})))
converges stochastically to
F_{X}(x_{0})
for
n\longrightarrow\infty
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\sqrt{n}\big(\hat{\Phi}_{n}(x_{0})-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})\big)\leq v\big)\longrightarrow\

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in[0,1]}\{g_{\beta,c}(s)-vs\}\geq F_{X}(x_{0})\bigg)=\mathbb{P}\big(g_{\beta,c}^{*,\ell}(F_{X}(x_{0}))\leq v\big),

where the last equality is a consequence of the switch relation (Lemma
2.1
).
As this is true for every
v\in\mathbb{R}
, statements (ii) and (iii) now follow immediately.

\bullet
Statement (i) could be deduced by appropriately specifying and verifying the technical ingredients from
Theorem 2.2 in
Mallick et al. (2023)
, which itself follows the so-called direct approach along the lines of

Wright (1981)
. Here, however, we prove (i) based on the switch relation in line with the proof of (ii) and
(iii), as it highlights the occurence of the convergence rate of the inverse process, which also plays an important role in
Section
4
. Let us start by introducing the following functions

\displaystyle g\colon[-T,T]\times[-T,T]\to\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle\quad g(x,t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq t\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}\}},

\displaystyle f_{n}\colon[-T,T]\times\{0,1\}\times[-T,T]\to\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle\quad f_{n}(x,y,t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))g(x,t)

and let
r_{n}=(n/\delta_{n})^{\beta/(2\beta+1)}
. As in (
B.3
) and by noting that adding
expressions which are independent of
s
does not change the
location of the minimum of a function in
s
, we obtain by an addition of zero

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(r_{n}\big(\hat{\Phi}_{n}(x_{0})-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})\big)\leq v\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\operatorname*{argmin^{+}}_{s\in[-T,T]}\bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},s)-\frac{v}{nr_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i},s)\bigg\}\geq F_{n}^{-1}(F_{n}(x_{0}))\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\operatorname*{argmin^{+}}_{s\in[x_{0}-T,x_{0}+T]}\bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},x_{0}+s)-\frac{v}{nr_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i},x_{0}+s)\bigg\}\geq F_{n}^{-1}(F_{n}(x_{0}))-x_{0}\bigg).

Defining
E_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},t)]
for
t\in[-T,T]
,

a_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(n\delta_{n}^{2\beta})^{-1/(2\beta+1)}
and
b_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(n^{\beta+1}\delta_{n}^{\beta})^{1/(2\beta+1)}
,
an addition of zero and multiplying with
b_{n}
inside the
\operatorname*{argmin}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(r_{n}\big(\hat{\Phi}_{n}(x_{0})-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})\big)\leq v\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\operatorname*{argmin^{+}}_{s\in[x_{0}-T,x_{0}+T]}\bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},x_{0}+s)-E_{n}(x_{0}+s)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+E_{n}(x_{0}+s)-\frac{v}{nr_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i},x_{0}+s)\bigg\}-\big(F_{n}^{-1}(F_{n}(x_{0}))-x_{0}\big)\geq 0\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(a_{n}\operatorname*{argmin^{+}}_{s\in[a_{n}^{-1}(x_{0}-T),a_{n}^{-1}(x_{0}+T)]}\bigg\{\frac{b_{n}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}s)-E_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}s)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+b_{n}E_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}s)-v\frac{b_{n}}{nr_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i},x_{0}+a_{n}s)\bigg\}-\big(F_{n}^{-1}(F_{n}(x_{0}))-x_{0}\big)\geq 0\bigg).

By Lemma
B.1
, the sequence inside the
\operatorname*{argmin}
restricted to
[-S,S]
converges weakly in the space
\ell^{\infty}([-S,S])
to

\bigg(\sigma_{\Phi_{0}}\sqrt{p_{X}(x_{0})}Z(s)+\frac{1}{(\beta+1)!}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(0)p_{X}(x_{0})s^{\beta+1}-vp_{X}(x_{0})s\bigg)_{s\in[-S,S]},

for every
S>0
, as long as
(n\delta_{n}^{2\beta})\longrightarrow\infty
.
From Proposition
B.3
, we
then obtain convergence in distribution of the respective
\operatorname*{argmin}
’s and by Lemma A.2 of
Cattaneo et al. (2024)
,
the
\operatorname*{argmin}
of this process has a continuous distribution function. Further,
a_{n}^{-1}\big(F_{n}^{-1}(F_{n}(x_{0}))-x_{0}\big)
converges stochastically to zero for
n\longrightarrow\infty
and thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(r_{n}\big(\hat{\Phi}_{n}(x_{0})

\displaystyle-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})\big)\leq v\big)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg\{\sigma_{\Phi_{0}}\sqrt{p_{X}(x_{0})}Z(s)+\frac{\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(0)p_{X}(x_{0})}{(\beta+1)!}s^{\beta+1}-vp_{X}(x_{0})s\bigg\}\geq 0\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{p_{X}(x_{0})}\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg\{\sigma_{\Phi_{0}}Z(s)+\frac{\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(0)}{p_{X}(x_{0})^{\beta}(\beta+1)!}s^{\beta+1}-vs\bigg\}\geq 0\bigg),

as
n\longrightarrow\infty
and by the switch relation (Lemma
2.1
), for every
v\in\mathbb{R}
,

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}\big(\hat{\Phi}_{n}(x_{0})-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})\big)\leq v\Big)\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}\big(f_{\beta}^{*,\ell}(0)\leq v\big)\quad\text{as }n\longrightarrow\infty.

∎

B.3
Auxiliary results for the proof of Theorem
3.2

For the results related to the proof of Theorem
3.2
(i), let us recall the definitions

\displaystyle g\colon[-T,T]\times[-T,T]\to\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle\quad g(x,t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq t\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}\}},

\displaystyle f_{n}\colon[-T,T]\times\{0,1\}\times[-T,T]\to\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle\quad f_{n}(x,y,t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))g(x,t)

and
E_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},t)]
for every
t\in[-T,T]
.
Furthermore, let
\beta\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, let

r_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{\beta/(2\beta+1)},\qquad a_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(n\delta_{n}^{2\beta})^{-1/(2\beta+1)},\qquad b_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(n^{\beta+1}\delta_{n}^{\beta})^{1/(2\beta+1)}

and let
Z(s)
denote a standard two-sided Brownian motion on
\mathbb{R}
. We also define the stochastic processes

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{b_{n}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}s)-E_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}s)\big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=b_{n}E_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}s),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=v\frac{b_{n}}{nr_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i},x_{0}+a_{n}s),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}^{1}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sqrt{\Phi_{0}(0)(1-\Phi_{0}(0))p_{X}(x_{0})}Z(s),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}^{2}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{(\beta+1)!}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(0)p_{X}(x_{0})s^{\beta+1},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}^{3}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=vp_{X}(x_{0})s

and set

\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s)+\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s)-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}(s),\quad\mathfrak{Z}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathfrak{Z}^{1}(s)+\mathfrak{Z}^{2}(s)-\mathfrak{Z}^{3}(s)

for
s\in[a_{n}^{-1}(x_{0}-T),a_{n}^{-1}(x_{0}+T)]
. Moreover, let

\hat{s}_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname*{argmin^{+}}_{s\in[a_{n}^{-1}(x_{0}-T),a_{n}^{-1}(x_{0}+T)]}\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(s)\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{s}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\mathfrak{Z}(s)

denote the minimizers of
\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(s)
and
\mathfrak{Z}(s)
respectively.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{N}
,
x_{0}
an interior point of
\mathcal{X}
and assume
\Phi_{0}
to be
\beta
-times continuously
differentiable in a neighborhood of
0
with the
\beta
th derivative being the first non-vanishing derivative in
0
.
Then, as long as
n\delta_{n}^{2\beta}\longrightarrow\infty
,

(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(s))_{s\in[-S,S]}\longrightarrow_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathfrak{Z}(s))_{s\in[-S,S]}\ \text{ in }\ell^{\infty}([-S,S])

for every
S>0
.

Let
S>0
be fixed but arbitrary and denote
\|f\|_{[-S,S]}:=\sup_{s\in[-S,S]}|f(s)|
for any continuous
f:[-S,S]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
.

Claim I:

\|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}-\mathfrak{Z}^{2}\|_{[-S,S]}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0
.

Proof of Claim I.
By a Taylor expansion with Lagrange remainder of

\Phi_{n}(x)
around
x_{0}
up to order
\beta
, there exists
\xi_{n}(x)
between
x_{0}
and
x
such that

\Phi_{n}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})=\frac{1}{\beta!}\Phi_{n}^{(\beta)}(\xi_{n}(x))(x-x_{0})^{\beta}=\delta_{n}^{\beta}\frac{1}{\beta!}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(\delta_{n}\xi_{n}(x))(x-x_{0})^{\beta}.

Thus, by using
b_{n}\delta_{n}^{\beta}a_{n}^{\beta+1}=1
,

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[-S,S]}|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s)-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s)|

\displaystyle=\sup_{s\in[-S,S]}\bigg|b_{n}\int_{x_{0}}^{x_{0}+a_{n}s}\frac{\delta_{n}^{\beta}}{\beta!}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(\delta_{n}\xi_{n}(x))(x-x_{0})^{\beta}p_{X}(x)dx-\frac{\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(0)p_{X}(x_{0})}{(\beta+1)!}s^{\beta+1}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beta!}\sup_{s\in[-S,S]}\bigg|b_{n}\delta_{n}^{\beta}a_{n}^{\beta+1}\int_{0}^{s}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(\delta_{n}\xi_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}x))x^{\beta}p_{X}(x_{0}+a_{n}x)dx-\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(0)p_{X}(x_{0})\int_{0}^{s}x^{\beta}dx\bigg|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beta!}\sup_{s\in[-S,S]}\bigg|\int_{0}^{s}\big(\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(\delta_{n}\xi_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}x))p_{X}(x_{0}+a_{n}x)-\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(0)p_{X}(x_{0})\big)x^{\beta}dx\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2S^{\beta+1}}{\beta!}\Big\|\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(\delta_{n}\xi_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}))p_{X}(x_{0}+a_{n}\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}})-\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(0)p_{X}(x_{0})\Big\|_{[-S,S]}

which converges to zero as
n\longrightarrow\infty
by continuity of
\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}
and
p_{X}
, the convergence
a_{n}\longrightarrow 0
and
\delta_{n}\longrightarrow 0
, as well as
\xi_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}})\in[-S,S]
.

Claim II:

\|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}-\mathfrak{Z}^{3}\|_{[-S,S]}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0
.

Proof of Claim II.

Define

g_{n,s}\colon[-S,S]\to\mathbb{R},\quad g_{n,s}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=va_{n}^{-1}\big(\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}\}}\big)

for every
s\in[-S,S]
, set
\mathcal{G}_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{g_{n,s}\mid s\in[-S,S]\}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and note that

\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}(s)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{n,s}(X_{i}).

From

\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}(s)]=va_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}\}}]=va_{n}^{-1}\big(F_{X}(x_{0}+a_{n}s)-F_{X}(x_{0})\big),

we deduce with
s_{n}^{*}\in[-S,S]
denoting the maximizier of the function inside the subsequent supremum,

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[-S,S]}\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}(s)]-vp_{X}(x_{0})s\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|va_{n}^{-1}(F_{X}(x_{0}+a_{n}s_{n}^{*})-F_{X}(x_{0}))-vp_{X}(x_{0})s_{n}^{*}\Big|

(B.5)

\displaystyle=|vs_{n}^{*}|\Big|\frac{1}{a_{n}s_{n}^{*}}(F_{X}(x_{0}+a_{n}s_{n}^{*})-F_{X}(x_{0}))-p_{X}(x_{0})\Big|\mathds{1}_{\{s_{n}^{*}\not=0\}}

\displaystyle\leq|vS|\Big|\frac{1}{a_{n}s_{n}^{*}}(F_{X}(x_{0}+a_{n}s_{n}^{*})-F_{X}(x_{0}))-p_{X}(x_{0})\Big|\mathds{1}_{\{s_{n}^{*}\not=0\}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow 0\quad\text{as }\,n\longrightarrow\infty

by the fundamental theorem of calculus.

Now we bound the
\nu
-bracketing number

N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{G}_{n},L^{1}(P_{X}))
. To this end, let
\nu>0
be arbitrary, set
N(\nu)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{2S}{\nu}2vp_{X}(x_{0})

and define for
i=1,\dots,\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor
,

s_{0}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-S,\qquad s_{i}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=s_{i-1}+\frac{\nu}{2vp_{X}(x_{0})},\qquad s_{\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor+1}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=S.

Then,
-S=s_{0}<s_{1}<\cdots<s_{\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor+1}=S
and

s_{i}-s_{i-1}\leq\frac{\nu}{2vp_{X}(x_{0})}
for
1\leq i\leq\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor+1
and for every

s\in[-S,S]
, there exists
i\in\{1,\dots,\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor+1\}
such that
s_{i-1}\leq s\leq s_{i}
.
Consequently, we have
g_{n,s_{i-1}}(x)\leq g_{n,s}(x)\leq g_{n,s_{i}}(x)
for every
x\in\mathbb{R}
and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|g_{n,s_{i}}(x)-g_{n,s_{i-1}}(x)|dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}g_{n,s_{i}}(x)-vs_{i}p_{X}(x_{0})+vs_{i-1}p_{X}(x_{0})-g_{n,s_{i-1}}(x)dP_{X}(x)+v(s_{i}-s_{i-1})p_{X}(x_{0})

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{s\in[-S,S]}|\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}(s)]-vp_{X}(x_{0})s|+\frac{\nu}{2}.

By (
B.5
),
\|\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}]-\mathfrak{Z}^{3}\|_{[-S,S]}<\nu/4

for
n
large enough, whence
[g_{n,s_{i-1}},g_{n,s_{i}}]_{i=1,\dots,\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor+1}

define
\nu
-brackets for
\mathcal{G}_{n}
with respect to
L^{1}(P_{X})
and

N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{G}_{n},L^{1}(P_{X}))\leq\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor+1\leq 1+\frac{2S}{\nu}2vp_{X}(x_{0})

for
n
sufficiently large. Moreover,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}(g_{n,s}(X))\leq v^{2}a_{n}^{-2}\big(\mathbb{E}[(\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}\}})^{2}]\big)\leq 2v^{2}a_{n}^{-2}

(B.6)

for every
s\in[-S,S]
.
By definition of
\mathcal{G}_{n}
,

\big\|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}-\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}]\big\|_{[-S,S]}=\sup_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{n}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i})-\mathbb{E}[g(X)]\bigg|.

Therefore, for every
\varepsilon>0
, we obtain with the
(\varepsilon/2)
-brackets
g_{n}^{1},\dots,g_{n}^{N(\varepsilon/2)}\in\mathcal{G}_{n}
by the union bound, Chebychev’s inequality and (
B.6
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\big\|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}-\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}]\big\|_{[-S,S]}\geq\varepsilon\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\max_{j=1,\dots,N(\varepsilon/2)}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{n}^{j}(X_{i})-\mathbb{E}[g_{n}^{j}(X)]\Big|\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\big(N(\varepsilon/2)+1\big)\frac{8}{\varepsilon^{2}}v^{2}\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}^{4\beta}}{n^{2\beta-1}}\Big)^{1/(2\beta+1)}\longrightarrow 0

as
n\longrightarrow\infty
. Together with (
B.5
), this reveals

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}-\mathfrak{Z}^{3}\|_{[-S,S]}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}-\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}]\big\|_{[-S,S]}+\big\|\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}]-\mathfrak{Z}^{3}\big\|_{[-S,S]}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0\quad\text{as }n\longrightarrow\infty

Claim III:

(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s))_{s\in[-S,S]}\longrightarrow_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathfrak{Z}^{1}(s))_{s\in[-S,S]}
in
\ell^{\infty}([-S,S])
.

Proof of Claim III.
By Theorem 1.5.4 in
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
, it is sufficient to show that the sequence of
stochastic processes
\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}
is asymptotically tight and
that for every finite subset
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{k}\}\subset[-S,S]
, the marginals

(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s_{1}),\dots,\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s_{k}))
converge weakly to
(\mathfrak{Z}^{1}(s_{1}),\dots,\mathfrak{Z}^{1}(s_{k}))
.

Convergence of finite-dimensional distributions.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
be arbitrary, let
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{k}\}\subset[-S,S]
denote an arbitrary finite subset of

[-S,S]
and note that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s_{1})\\
\vdots\\
\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s_{k})\end{pmatrix}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{b_{n}}{n}\begin{pmatrix}f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}s_{1})-\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}s_{1})]\\
\vdots\\
f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}s_{k})-\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}s_{k})]\end{pmatrix}.

As a shorthand notation, let us introduce

\displaystyle V_{i}^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{b_{n}}{n}\begin{pmatrix}f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},s_{1})\\
\vdots\\
f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},s_{k})\end{pmatrix}

for
i=1,\dots,n
.
Note that

\|V_{i}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}\leq k\frac{b_{n}^{2}}{n^{2}}=k(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n})^{2\beta/(2\beta+1)}
by definition of
f_{n}

and
b_{n}
, hence for every
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\|V_{i}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}_{\{\|V_{i}^{n}\|_{2}>\varepsilon\}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq k\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)^{2\beta/(2\beta+1)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathds{1}_{\{\|V_{i}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}>\varepsilon^{2}\}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq k\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)^{2\beta/(2\beta+1)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathds{1}_{\{k>(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}})^{2\beta/(2\beta+1)}\varepsilon^{2}\}}\big]

\displaystyle=ka_{n}^{-1}\mathds{1}_{\{k>(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}})^{2\beta/(2\beta+1)}\varepsilon^{2}\}}\longrightarrow 0\quad\text{as }n\longrightarrow\infty,

where we used
n/\delta_{n}\longrightarrow\infty
. For the sum of the covariance
matrices of
V_{i}
, note that for
j,\ell\in\{1,\dots,k\}
,

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{Cov}(V_{i}^{n})\bigg)_{j\ell}\hskip-3.6pt=\frac{b_{n}^{2}}{n}\big(\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X,Y^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}s_{j})f_{n}(X,Y^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}s_{\ell})]-E_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}s_{j})E_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}s_{\ell})\big).

Recall from Claim I that
b_{n}E_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}s)=\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s)\longrightarrow\frac{1}{(\beta+1)!}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(0)p_{X}(x_{0})s^{\beta+1}
for any
s\in[-S,S]
as
n\longrightarrow\infty
, whence

\frac{b_{n}^{2}}{n}E_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}s_{j})E_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}s_{\ell})=\frac{1}{n}\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s_{j})\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s_{\ell})\longrightarrow 0\quad\text{as }n\longrightarrow\infty.

Observe further that

\displaystyle f_{n}(X,Y^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}s_{j})f_{n}(X,Y^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}s_{\ell})

\displaystyle\qquad=(Y^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}\big(\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s_{j}\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}\}}\big)\big(\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s_{\ell}\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}\}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad=(Y^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}\big(\mathds{1}_{\{x_{0}<X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}\}}+\mathds{1}_{\{x_{0}-a_{n}\min\{-s_{j},-s_{\ell}\}<X\leq x_{0}\}}\big)

and consequently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X,Y^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}s_{j})f_{n}(X,Y^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}s_{\ell})]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\big[(Y^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}\big(\mathds{1}_{\{x_{0}<X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}\}}+\mathds{1}_{\{x_{0}-a_{n}\min\{-s_{j},-s_{\ell}\}<X\leq x_{0}\}}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathds{1}_{\{s_{j},s_{\ell}>0\}}\mathbb{E}\big[(Y^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}\mathds{1}_{\{x_{0}<X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}\}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mathds{1}_{\{s_{j},s_{\ell}<0\}}\mathbb{E}\big[(Y^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}\mathds{1}_{\{x_{0}-a_{n}\min\{|s_{j}|,|s_{\ell}|\}<X\leq x_{0}\}}\big].

From now on, we will only consider the case
s_{j},s_{\ell}>0
as the case
s_{j},s_{\ell}<0
follows analogously.
Note first that by the tower property of conditional expectation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(Y^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}\mathds{1}_{\{x_{0}<X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}\}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\big[\big((1-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}\Phi_{n}(X)+(\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}(1-\Phi_{n}(X))\big)\mathds{1}_{\{x_{0}<X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}\}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\big[\big(\Phi_{n}(X)(1-2\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))+\Phi_{n}(x_{0})^{2}\big)\mathds{1}_{\{x_{0}<X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}\}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\big[\Phi_{n}(X)(1-2\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\mathds{1}_{\{x_{0}<X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}\}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Phi_{n}(x_{0})^{2}(F_{X}(x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\})-F_{X}(x_{0})).

Now, because of
\frac{b_{n}^{2}}{n}=a_{n}^{-1}
, we obtain

\frac{b_{n}^{2}}{n}\Phi_{n}(x_{0})^{2}\big(F_{X}(x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\})-F_{X}(x_{0})\big)\longrightarrow\Phi_{0}(0)^{2}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}p_{X}(x_{0})

as
n\longrightarrow\infty
. Defining
J_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{x_{0}}^{t}\Phi_{n}(x)p_{X}(x)dx
,
we further have

\displaystyle\frac{b_{n}^{2}}{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\Phi_{n}(X)(1-2\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\mathds{1}_{\{x_{0}<X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}\}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad=a_{n}^{-1}(1-2\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))J_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\})

and by a Taylor expansion with Lagrange remainder of
J_{n}(t)
around
x_{0}
, we obtain for a suitable intermediate point

\eta_{n}
between
x_{0}
and
x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}
by the fundamental theorem of calculus

\displaystyle a_{n}^{-1}

\displaystyle(1-2\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))J_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,a_{n}^{-1}(1-2\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))(J_{n}(x_{0})+J_{n}^{\prime}(\eta_{n})a_{n}\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(1-2\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))J_{n}^{\prime}(\eta_{n})\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,(1-2\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\Phi_{n}(\eta_{n})p_{X}(\eta_{n})\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}\longrightarrow(1-2\Phi_{0}(0))\Phi_{0}(0)p_{X}(x_{0})\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\},

where we used that
\eta_{n}\longrightarrow x_{0}
as
n\longrightarrow\infty
. Combining the previous
calculations, we see

\displaystyle\bigg(

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{Cov}(V_{i}^{n})\bigg)_{j\ell}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt\longrightarrow\big((1-2\Phi_{0}(0))\Phi_{0}(0)p_{X}(x_{0})\min\{|s_{j}|,|s_{\ell}|\}+\Phi_{0}(0)^{2}\min\{|s_{j}|,|s_{\ell}|\}p_{X}(x_{0})\big)\mathds{1}_{\{s_{j}s_{\ell}>0\}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\longrightarrow}\ =\Phi_{0}(0)(1-\Phi_{0}(0))p_{X}(x_{0})\min\{|s_{j}|,|s_{\ell}|\}\mathds{1}_{\{s_{j}s_{\ell}>0\}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\longrightarrow}\ =\operatorname{Cov}\big(\sqrt{\Phi_{0}(0)(1-\Phi_{0}(0))p_{X}(x_{0})}Z(s_{j}),\sqrt{\Phi_{0}(0)(1-\Phi_{0}(0))p_{X}(x_{0})}Z(s_{\ell})\big)

\displaystyle\phantom{\longrightarrow}\ =\operatorname{Cov}(\mathfrak{Z}^{1}(s_{j}),\mathfrak{Z}^{1}(s_{\ell})).

Finally, the Lindeberg-Feller central limit theorem yields

(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s_{1}),\dots,\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s_{k}))\longrightarrow_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathfrak{Z}^{1}(s_{1}),\dots,\mathfrak{Z}^{1}(s_{k}))\quad\text{as }n\longrightarrow\infty.

Asymptotic tightness.
By convergence of the finite dimensional distributions, it is sufficient by
Theorem 1.5.7 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
to prove asymptotic uniform equicontinuity in probability. For this, let

\Delta>0
be fixed but arbitrary and note that by Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{|s-t|<\eta}|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s)-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(t)|>\Delta\bigg)\leq\frac{1}{\Delta}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|s-t|<\eta}|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s)-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(t)|\bigg].

(B.7)

Define

f_{n,s,t}\colon[-S,S]\times\{0,1\}\to\mathbb{R},\quad f_{n,s,t}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\big(\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}t\}}\big)

for
s,t\in[-S,S]
, and
\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{f_{n,s,t}\mid s,t\in[-S,S],|s-t|<\eta\}
. For any

\varepsilon_{n}>0
and
M_{n}>0
satisfying
\mathbb{E}[f^{2}]<\varepsilon_{n}^{2}
and
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq M_{n}
for every

f\in\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta}
, Theorem 2.14.17’ of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
reveals for a universal constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|s-t|<\eta}|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s)-

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(t)|\bigg]=b_{n}n^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{f_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n})-\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n})]\bigg|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq Cb_{n}n^{-1/2}J_{[]}\big(\varepsilon_{n},\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\bigg(1+\frac{J_{[]}\big(\varepsilon_{n},\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)}{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}n^{1/2}}M_{n}\bigg)

(B.8)

with

J_{[]}\big(\varepsilon_{n},\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{0}^{\varepsilon_{n}}\sqrt{1+\log(N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}}))}d\nu
.
It remains to specify
M_{n}
,
\varepsilon_{n}
and a bound for the entropy with bracketing. For arbitrary
f\in\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta}
, there exist
s,t\in[-S,S]
, satisfying
|s-t|<\eta
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f(X,Y^{n})^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[(\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}t\}})^{2}]=|F_{X}(x_{0}+a_{n}s)-F_{X}(x_{0}+a_{n}t)|\leq a_{n}\eta\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}

and

\|f\|_{\infty}=\|f_{n,s,t}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
.
Thus, with
\varepsilon_{n}=\sqrt{a_{n}\eta\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}}
and
M_{n}=1
, (
B.8
) is equal to

b_{n}n^{-1/2}J_{[]}\big(\sqrt{a_{n}\eta\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}},\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\bigg(1+\frac{J_{[]}\big(\sqrt{a_{n}\eta\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}},\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)}{a_{n}\eta\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{n}}\bigg).

By Lemma
G.7
(i), it follows that for some constant
K>0
independent from the variable parameters in the
following expressions and which may change from line to line,

N_{[]}\big(\nu,\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\leq N_{[]}\big(\nu,\mathcal{F}_{n,2S},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\leq a_{n}^{2}\frac{K}{\nu^{4}}.

Thus, by utilizing that
\frac{d}{dx}x(\log(K/x^{4})+4)=\log(K/x^{4})
,

\displaystyle J_{[]}\big(\sqrt{a_{n}\eta\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}},\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\leq K\int_{0}^{\sqrt{a_{n}\eta\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}}}\log\Big(a_{n}^{2}\frac{K}{\nu^{4}}\Big)d\nu\leq K\sqrt{a_{n}\eta}\log\Big(\frac{K}{\eta^{2}}\Big)

and Claim III follows from (
B.7
) together with

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|s-t|<\eta}|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(s)-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}(t)|\bigg]\leq\limsup_{n\to\infty}Kb_{n}n^{-1/2}\sqrt{a_{n}\eta}\log\Big(\frac{K}{\eta^{2}}\Big)=K\sqrt{\eta}\log\Big(\frac{K}{\eta^{2}}\Big).

The assertion now follows from the fact that
\mathfrak{Z}_{n}=\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{1}+\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}
as well as that

\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}
and
\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{3}
converge to nonrandom functions.
∎

Lemma B.2
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
B.1
, the minimizers
\hat{s}_{n}
of

\mathfrak{Z}_{n}
form a tight sequence.

Let
s_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s)
and note that
s_{n}=0
, which follows from

\displaystyle E_{n}(x_{0}+a_{n}s)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(Y^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))(\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}\}})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[(\Phi_{n}(X)-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))(\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}\}})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[|\Phi_{n}(X)-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})||\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}\}}|],

where we used monotonicity of
\Phi_{n}
. Next, fix some neighborhood
U(0)
of zero and note that, as long as
n
is large enough,

\inf_{x\in U(0)}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(x)>0

and
p_{X}>0
on its
support
[-T,T]
. Assuming
s\geq 0
for the moment, a Taylor expansion of
\Phi_{n}
around
x_{0}
reveals for some
\xi_{n}
between
X
and
x_{0}
and some
constant
C>0
, which may change from line to line, that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s)

\displaystyle=b_{n}\mathbb{E}[(\Phi_{n}(X)-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))(\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}\}})]

\displaystyle=b_{n}\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{n}^{(\beta)}(\xi_{n})(X-x_{0})^{\beta}(\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}\}})]

\displaystyle\geq Cb_{n}\delta_{n}^{\beta}\mathbb{E}[(X-x_{0})^{\beta}(\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}\}})]

\displaystyle=Cb_{n}\delta_{n}^{\beta}\int_{0}^{a_{n}s}x^{\beta}p_{X}(x_{0}+x)dx

\displaystyle\geq Cb_{n}\delta_{n}^{\beta}a_{n}^{\beta+1}s^{\beta+1}=Cs^{\beta+1}.

By similar arguments, the same result holds for
s\leq 0
. To show uniform tightness of
\hat{s}_{n}
, we use a slicing
argument similar to the proof of Theorem 3.2.5 in
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
. For
j\in\mathbb{N}
, define the slices

S_{j,n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\big\{s\in\mathbb{R}\mid 2^{j-1}<|s|^{\beta+1}\leq 2^{j}\big\}.

Using that
\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(s_{n})-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(\hat{s}_{n})\geq 0
by the property of
\hat{s}_{n}
, we obtain by
\sigma
-subadditivity, as well as

\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(s_{n})=0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|\hat{s}_{n}|>2^{K}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=K+1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{s\in S_{j,n}}(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(s_{n})-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(s))\geq 0\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=K+1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{s\in S_{j,n}}(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s)-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(s)-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s))\geq 0\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=K+1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{s\in S_{j,n}}(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s)-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(s))\geq\inf_{s\in S_{j,n}}\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}(s)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=K+1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big(\|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}\|_{S_{j,n}}\geq C2^{j-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{C}\sum_{j=K+1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{j}}\mathbb{E}[\|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}\|_{S_{j,n}}],

where we used Markov’s inequality in the last step.
Let us now define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}_{n,j}^{1}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigg\|\frac{b_{n}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}})-\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},x_{0}+a_{n}\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}})]\bigg\|_{S_{j,n}},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}_{n,j}^{3}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigg\|v\frac{b_{n}}{nr_{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(X_{i},x_{0}+a_{n}\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}})\bigg\|_{S_{j,n}}

and note that

\mathbb{E}[\|\mathfrak{Z}_{n}-\mathfrak{Z}_{n}^{2}\|_{S_{j,n}}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n,j}^{1}]+\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n,j}^{3}].

As an immediate consequence, we have

\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n,j}^{3}]\leq v(n\delta_{n}^{2\beta})^{\frac{1}{2\beta+1}}\mathbb{E}\mathds{1}_{(x_{0},x_{0}+a_{n}S]}\leq v\|p_{X}\|_{[-T,T]}.

Define

f_{n,s}\colon[-S,S]\times\{0,1\}\to\mathbb{R},\quad f_{n,s}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\big(\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}\}}\big)

for
s\in\mathbb{R}
and set
\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{f_{n,s}\mid s\in\mathbb{R},2^{j}<|s|^{\beta+1}\leq 2^{j+1}\}
.
For any
\varepsilon_{n}>0
and
M_{n}>0
satisfying
\mathbb{E}[f^{2}]<\varepsilon_{n}^{2}
and
\|f\|_{\infty}\leq M_{n}

for every
f\in\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta}
, Theorem 2.14.17’ of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
reveals for a universal constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n,j}^{1}]

\displaystyle=b_{n}n^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{f_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n})-\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n})]\bigg|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq Cb_{n}n^{-1/2}J_{[]}\big(\varepsilon_{n},\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\bigg(1+\frac{J_{[]}\big(\varepsilon_{n},\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)}{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}n^{1/2}}M_{n}\bigg)

(B.9)

with

J_{[]}\big(\varepsilon_{n},\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{0}^{\varepsilon_{n}}\sqrt{1+\log(N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}}))}d\nu
. It
remains to specify
M_{n}
,
\varepsilon_{n}
and a bound for the entropy with bracketing. For arbitrary

f\in\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta}
, there exist
s\in[-S,S]
, satisfying
|s|^{\beta+1}\leq 2^{j+1}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f(X,Y^{n})^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[(\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq x_{0}\}})^{2}]=|F_{X}(x_{0}+a_{n}s)-F_{X}(x_{0})|\leq a_{n}2^{(j+1)/(\beta+1)}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}

and
\|f\|_{\infty}=\|f_{n,s}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Thus, with

\varepsilon_{n}=\sqrt{a_{n}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}}2^{\frac{j+1}{2(\beta+1)}}
and

M_{n}=1
, (
B.9
) equals

\displaystyle b_{n}n^{-1/2}J_{[]}\big(\sqrt{a_{n}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}}2^{\frac{j+1}{2(\beta+1)}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\bigg(1+\frac{J_{[]}\big(\sqrt{a_{n}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}}2^{\frac{j+1}{2(\beta+1)}},\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)}{\sqrt{a_{n}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}}2^{\frac{j+1}{2(\beta+1)}}\sqrt{n}}\bigg).

By Lemma
G.7
(ii), it follows that for some constant
L>0
independent from the variable parameters in
the following expressions and which may changes from line to line,

N_{[]}\big(\nu,\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\leq 2^{\frac{j+1}{\beta+1}}a_{n}\frac{L}{\nu^{2}},

Thus, by utilizing that
\frac{d}{dx}x(\log(K/x^{2})+2)=\log(K/x^{2})
,

\displaystyle J_{[]}\big(\sqrt{a_{n}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}}2^{\frac{j+1}{2(\beta+1)}},\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\leq L\int_{0}^{\sqrt{a_{n}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}}2^{\frac{j+1}{2(\beta+1)}}}\log\Big(2^{\frac{j+1}{\beta+1}}a_{n}\frac{L}{\nu^{2}}\Big)d\nu\leq L\sqrt{a_{n}}2^{\frac{j+1}{2(\beta+1)}}

and we have

\mathbb{E}[\mathfrak{Z}_{n,j}^{1}]\leq Lb_{n}n^{-1/2}\sqrt{a_{n}}2^{\frac{j+1}{2(\beta+1)}}=L2^{\frac{j+1}{2(\beta+1)}}.

Summarizing, we have shown

\mathbb{P}\big(|\hat{s}_{n}|>2^{K}\big)\leq v\|p_{X}\|_{[-T,T]}\frac{4L}{C}\sum_{j=K+1}^{\infty}\frac{2^{\frac{j}{2(\beta+1)}}}{2^{j}}=v\|p_{X}\|_{[-T,T]}\frac{4L}{C}\sum_{j=K+1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{j\frac{(2\beta+1)}{2(\beta+1)}}}\longrightarrow 0

as
K\longrightarrow\infty
and the assertion follows.
∎

Proposition B.3
.

Under the same assumptions as in Lemma
B.1
, the sequence of minimizers
\hat{s}_{n}
of

\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(s)
converges weakly to the minimizer
\hat{s}
of
\mathfrak{Z}(s)
for
n\longrightarrow\infty
.

From Lemma
B.1
, we have for every compact set
\mathcal{K}\subset\mathbb{R}
that
(\mathfrak{Z}_{n}(s))_{s\in\mathcal{K}}

converges weakly to
(\mathfrak{Z}(s))_{s\in\mathcal{K}}
in
\ell^{\infty}(\mathcal{K})
. Moreover, the sample paths

s\mapsto\mathfrak{Z}(s)
are continuous and
\hat{s}
is unique a.s. and tight (cf.
Wright (1981)
). By
Lemma
B.2
,
\hat{s}_{n}
is uniformly tight and consequently, by Theorem 3.2.2 of

van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
,

\hat{s}_{n}\longrightarrow_{\mathcal{L}}\hat{s}
as
n\longrightarrow\infty
.
∎

For the results related to the proof of Theorem
3.2
(ii) and (iii), let us recall the definitions

h_{n}\colon[-T,T]\times\{0,1\}\times[-T,T]\to\mathbb{R},\quad h_{n}(x,y,t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq t\}}

and
H_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{E}[h_{n}(X,Y^{n},t)]
for every
t\in[-T,T]
. Further, let
(W(s))_{s\in[0,1]}
denote a standard
Brownian motion on
[0,1]
, define the stochastic processes

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},F_{X}^{-1}(s))-H_{n}(F_{X}^{-1}(s))\big)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{2}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sqrt{n}H_{n}(F_{X}^{-1}(s))

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{3}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=v\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathds{1}_{\{X_{i}\leq F_{X}^{-1}(s)\}}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}^{1}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sqrt{\Phi_{0}(0)(1-\Phi_{0}(0))}B(s)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}_{c}^{2}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sqrt{c}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(0)\mathbb{E}\big[(X-x_{0})^{\beta}\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(s)\}}\big]

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}^{3}(s)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=vs

and set for
s\in[0,1]
,

\mathfrak{W}_{n}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(s)+\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{2}(s)-\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{3}(s),\quad\mathfrak{W}_{c}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathfrak{W}^{1}(s)+\mathfrak{W}_{c}^{2}(s)-\mathfrak{W}^{3}(s).

Further, we redefine

\hat{s}_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname*{argmin^{+}}_{s\in[0,1]}\mathfrak{W}_{n}(s)
and
\hat{s}_{c}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in[0,1]}\mathfrak{W}_{c}(s)
from the previous
proofs to now denote the minimizers of
\mathfrak{W}_{n}
and
\mathfrak{W}_{c}
respectively.

Lemma B.4
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{N}
,
x_{0}
an interior point of
\mathcal{X}
and assume
\Phi_{0}
to be
\beta
-times continuously
differentiable in a neighborhood of
0
with the
\beta
th derivative being the first non-vanishing derivative in
0
.
Then,

\mathfrak{W}_{n}\longrightarrow_{\mathcal{L}}\mathfrak{W}_{c}\ \text{ in }\,\ell^{\infty}([0,1])

as

n\longrightarrow\infty
and
n\delta_{n}^{2\beta}\longrightarrow c\in[0,\infty)
.

Claim I:

\|\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{2}-\mathfrak{W}_{c}^{2}\|_{[0,1]}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0
.

Proof of Claim I.
By assumption on
\Phi_{n}
,
a Taylor expansion of
\Phi_{n}
around
x_{0}
of order
\beta
with Lagrange remainder yields the existence of some

\xi_{n}
between
x
and
x_{0}
such that

\Phi_{n}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})=\Phi_{n}^{(\beta)}(\xi_{n})(x-x_{0})^{\beta}=\delta_{n}^{\beta}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(\delta_{n}\xi_{n})(x-x_{0})^{\beta}.

(B.10)

From
n\delta_{n}^{2\beta}\longrightarrow c
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,1]}|\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{2}(s)-\mathfrak{W}_{c}^{2}(s)|

\displaystyle=\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\big|\mathbb{E}\big[n^{1/2}\delta_{n}^{\beta}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(\delta_{n}\xi_{n})(X-x_{0})^{\beta}\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(s)\}}\big]-\mathfrak{W}_{c}^{2}(s)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\big|n^{1/2}\delta_{n}^{\beta}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(\delta_{n}\xi_{n})-\sqrt{c}\Phi_{0}^{(\beta)}(0)\big||X-x_{0}|^{\beta}\big]

which converges to zero, as
n\longrightarrow\infty
.

Claim II.

\|\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{3}-\mathfrak{W}^{3}\|_{[0,1]}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0
.

Proof of Claim II.
The convergence
\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{3}\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}vF_{X}\circ F_{X}^{-1}=\mathfrak{W}^{3}
in

\ell^{\infty}([0,1])
as

n\longrightarrow\infty
is exactly the classical Glivenko-Cantelli result.

Claim III:

\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}\longrightarrow_{\mathcal{L}}\mathfrak{W}^{1}
in
\ell^{\infty}([0,1])
.

Proof of Claim III.
By Theorem 1.5.4 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
, it is sufficient to show that the sequence of
stochastic processes
\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}
is asymptotically tight and
that for every finite subset
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{k}\}\subset[0,1]
, the marginals

(\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(s_{1}),\dots,\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(s_{k}))
converge weakly to
(\mathfrak{W}^{1}(s_{1}),\dots,\mathfrak{W}^{1}(s_{k}))
.

Convergence of finite-dimensional distributions.
For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{k}\}\subset[0,1]
denote some arbitrary finite subset of
[0,1]

and note that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(s_{1})\\
\vdots\\
\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(s_{k})\end{pmatrix}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\begin{pmatrix}h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},F_{X}^{-1}(s_{1}))-\mathbb{E}[h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},F_{X}^{-1}(s_{1}))]\\
\vdots\\
h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},F_{X}^{-1}(s_{k}))-\mathbb{E}[h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},F_{X}^{-1}(s_{k}))]\end{pmatrix}.

As a shorthand notation, let us introduce

\displaystyle V_{i}^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\begin{pmatrix}h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},F_{X}^{-1}(s_{1}))\\
\vdots\\
h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},F_{X}^{-1}(s_{k}))\end{pmatrix}

for
i=1,\dots,n
. Then,
\|V_{i}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}\leq k/n
by definition of
h_{n}
and we have for every

\varepsilon>0
,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\|V_{i}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}_{\{\|V_{i}^{n}\|_{2}>\varepsilon\}}]\leq\frac{k}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\{\|V_{i}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}>\varepsilon^{2}\}}]\leq\frac{k}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\{k>n\varepsilon^{2}\}}]=k\mathds{1}_{\{k>n\varepsilon^{2}\}}\longrightarrow 0

as
n\longrightarrow\infty
. As concerns the covariance matrices of
\sum_{i}V_{i}^{n}
, note that for

j,\ell\in\{1,\dots,k\}
,

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{Cov}(V_{i}^{n})\bigg)_{j\ell}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[(Y^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(s_{j})\}}\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(s_{\ell})\}}\big]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\big[(Y^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(s_{j})\}}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[(Y^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(s_{\ell})\}}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[(Y^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq\min{\{F_{X}^{-1}(s_{j}),F_{X}^{-1}(s_{\ell})\}}\}}\big]+\mathcal{O}(\delta_{n}^{2\beta})

by (
B.10
). For the remaining summand, we observe

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(Y^{n}-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(\min{\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}})\}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\big[((1-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}\Phi_{n}(X)+(\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))^{2}(1-\Phi_{n}(X)))\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(\min{\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}})\}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\big[(\Phi_{n}(X)-2\Phi_{n}(x_{0})\Phi_{n}(X)+\Phi_{n}(x_{0})^{2})\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(\min{\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}})\}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}\big[(\Phi_{0}(0)-\Phi_{0}(0)^{2})\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(\min{\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}})\}}\big]

as
n\longrightarrow\infty
by the theorem of dominated convergence. Thus,

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{Cov}(V_{i}^{n})\bigg)_{j\ell}\longrightarrow\,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[(\Phi_{0}(0)-\Phi_{0}(0)^{2})\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(\min{\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}})\}}\big]

\displaystyle=\Phi_{0}(0)(1-\Phi_{0}(0))\min{\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Cov}(\mathfrak{W}^{1}(s_{j}),\mathfrak{W}^{1}(s_{\ell})),

and by the Lindeberg-Feller central limit theorem, we conclude

(\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(s_{1}),\dots,\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(s_{k}))\longrightarrow_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathfrak{W}^{1}(s_{1}),\dots,\mathfrak{W}^{1}(s_{k}))\quad\text{as }n\longrightarrow\infty.

Asymptotic tightness.
By convergence of the finite dimensional distributions, it is sufficient by Theorem 1.5.7 of

van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
to prove asymptotic uniform equicontinuity in probability.
Define

h_{n,s,t}\colon[0,1]\times\{0,1\}\to\mathbb{R},\quad h_{n,s,t}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\big(\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq F_{X}^{-1}(s)\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq F_{X}^{-1}(t)\}}\big)

for
s,t\in[0,1]
and
\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{h_{n,s,t}\mid s,t\in[0,1],|s-t|<\eta\}
for
\eta>0
. For any

\varepsilon_{n}>0
and
M_{n}>0
satisfying
\mathbb{E}[h^{2}]<\varepsilon_{n}^{2}
and
\|h\|_{\infty}\leq M_{n}
for every

h\in\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta}
, Theorem 2.14.17’ in
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
provides for a universal constant
C>0
the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|s-t|<\eta}|\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(s)-\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(t)|\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{h_{n}\in\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n})-\mathbb{E}[h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n})]\bigg|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq CJ_{[]}\big(\varepsilon_{n},\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\bigg(1+\frac{J_{[]}\big(\varepsilon_{n},\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)}{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}n^{1/2}}M_{n}\bigg),

with

J_{[]}\big(\varepsilon_{n},\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{0}^{\varepsilon_{n}}\sqrt{1+\log(N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}}))}d\nu
.
For any
h\in\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta}
, there exist
s,t\in[0,1]
with
|s-t|<\eta
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[h(X,Y^{n})^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[(\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(s)\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(t)\}})^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\{F_{X}^{-1}(\min\{s,t\})<X\leq F_{X}^{-1}(\max\{s,t\})\}}]

\displaystyle=F_{X}(F_{X}^{-1}(\max\{s,t\}))-F_{X}(F_{X}^{-1}(\min\{s,t\}))=|s-t|<\eta

and
\|h\|_{\infty}\leq 1
.
Thus, by choosing
\varepsilon_{n}=\sqrt{\eta}
and
M_{n}=1
, we have

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|s-t|<\eta}|\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(s)-\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(t)|\bigg]\leq CJ_{[]}\big(\eta^{1/2},\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\bigg(1+\frac{J_{[]}\big(\sqrt{\eta},\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)}{\eta\sqrt{n}}\bigg).

By Lemma
G.7
(iii), it follows that for some constant
K>0
which does not depend on
the variables in the respective expressions and which may change from line to line,

N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}}))\leq N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{H}_{n,1},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}}))\leq\frac{K}{\nu^{4}}.

Thus, by utilizing that
\frac{d}{dx}x(\log(K/x^{4})+4)=\log(K/x^{4})
,

J_{[]}\big(\sqrt{\eta},\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\leq K\int_{0}^{\sqrt{\eta}}\log(K/\nu^{4})d\nu\leq K\sqrt{\eta}\log(1/\eta^{2})

and Claim III follows by Markov’s inequality and

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|s-t|<\eta}|\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(s)-\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}(t)|\bigg]\leq K\sqrt{\eta}\log(1/\eta^{2}).

The
assertion now follows from the fact that
\mathfrak{W}_{n}=\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{1}+\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{2}-\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{3}
as well as that

\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{2}
and
\mathfrak{W}_{n}^{3}
converge to nonrandom functions.
∎

Proposition B.5
.

Under the same conditions as in Lemma
B.4
, the sequence of minimizers
\hat{s}_{n}

of
(\mathfrak{W}_{n}(s))_{s\in[0,1]}
converges weakly to the minimizer
\hat{s}_{c}
of
(\mathfrak{W}_{c}(s))_{s\in[0,1]}
as

n\longrightarrow\infty
as long as we have
n\delta_{n}^{2\beta}\longrightarrow c\in[0,\infty)
.

By Lemma
B.4
,
\mathfrak{W}_{n}\longrightarrow_{\mathcal{L}}\mathfrak{W}
in

\ell^{\infty}([0,1])
as
n\longrightarrow\infty
. Further, the sample
paths
s\mapsto\mathfrak{W}_{c}(s)
are continuous and
\hat{s}_{c}
is unique by Theorem 2 of
Pimentel (2014)

and tight. As
\hat{s}_{n}\in[0,1]
is uniformly tight, Theorem 3.2.2 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
reveals

\hat{s}_{n}\longrightarrow_{\mathcal{L}}\hat{s}_{c}
as
n\longrightarrow\infty
.
∎

C
Remaining proofs of Section
4

In this section, the proof of Theorem
4.1
, Proposition
4.2
and Theorem
4.3
(i) are given.

C.1
Proof of Theorem
4.1

We will calculate lower bounds for

\inf_{T_{n}^{\delta}}\sup_{\Phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}}\Big(\sqrt{n}\wedge\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{1/3}\Big)\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\bigg[\int_{-T}^{T}\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x)-\Phi(x)\big|dx\bigg]

separately for both
\delta\geq n^{-1/2}
and
\delta<n^{-1/2}
, noting that

\max\{n^{-1/2},(\frac{n}{\delta})^{-1/3}\}=(\frac{n}{\delta})^{-1/3}
if and only if
\delta\geq n^{-1/2}
.

\bullet
Let us start with the case
\delta<n^{-1/2}
. Let
C\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{8T}}
, let

\eta_{n,\delta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=1/2-\delta T-Cn^{-1/2}
and define

\displaystyle\Phi_{0,n}\colon\mathbb{R}\to[0,1],

\displaystyle\quad\Phi_{0,n}|_{[-T,T]}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\delta(x+T)+\eta_{n,\delta}+2Cn^{-1/2},

\displaystyle\Phi_{1,n}\colon\mathbb{R}\to[0,1],

\displaystyle\quad\Phi_{1,n}|_{[-T,T]}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\delta(x+T)+\eta_{n,\delta},

where both functions are defined outside
[-T,T]
by their values at the respective boundaries.
Obviously,
\Phi_{0,n},\Phi_{1,n}\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}
and note that

\int_{-T}^{T}|\Phi_{0,n}(x)-\Phi_{1,n}(x)|dx=4TCn^{-1/2}.

Note further that for
n\geq 16(C+T)^{2}
and all
x\in[-T,T]
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{0,n}(x)

\displaystyle\geq\Phi_{1,n}(x)\geq\Phi_{1,n}(-T)=\eta_{n,\delta}\geq 1/2-n^{-1/2}(C+T)\geq 1/4,

\displaystyle 1-\Phi_{1,n}(x)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\Phi_{0,n}(x)\geq 1-\Phi_{0,n}(T)=1-2T\delta-\eta_{n,\delta}-2Cn^{-1/2}=\eta_{n,\delta}\geq 1/4.

Writing
P_{0,n}^{\otimes n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=P_{\Phi_{0,n}}^{\otimes n}
,

P_{1,n}^{\otimes n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=P_{\Phi_{1,n}}^{\otimes n}
, we have for
\alpha=4C^{2}

\displaystyle h^{2}\big(P_{0,n}^{\otimes n},P_{1,n}^{\otimes n}\big)\leq nh^{2}\big(P_{0,n},P_{1,n}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{n}{2}\int_{-T}^{T}\Big(\sqrt{\Phi_{0,n}(x)}-\sqrt{\Phi_{1,n}(x)}\Big)^{2}+\Big(\sqrt{1-\Phi_{0,n}(x)}-\sqrt{1-\Phi_{1,n}(x)}\Big)^{2}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{n}{2}\int_{-T}^{T}\bigg(\frac{\Phi_{0,n}(x)-\Phi_{1,n}(x)}{\sqrt{\Phi_{0,n}(x)}+\sqrt{\Phi_{1,n}(x)}}\bigg)^{2}+\bigg(\frac{\Phi_{0,n}(x)-\Phi_{1,n}(x)}{\sqrt{1-\Phi_{0,n}(x)}+\sqrt{1-\Phi_{1,n}(x)}}\bigg)^{2}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{n}{8}\int_{-T}^{T}(\Phi_{0,n}(x)-\Phi_{1,n}(x))^{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{\Phi_{1,n}(x)}+\frac{1}{1-\Phi_{0,n}(x)}\bigg)dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{n}{8}\int_{-T}^{T}8(\Phi_{0,n}(x)-\Phi_{1,n}(x))^{2}dP_{X}(x)=4C^{2}=\alpha<2.

From Chapter 2.2 in
Tsybakov (2009)
and Theorem 2.2 (ii) of
Tsybakov (2009)
, we have
