\inf_{T_{n}^{\delta}}\sup_{\Phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}}C\Big(\sqrt{n}\wedge\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{1/3}\Big)\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\bigg[\int_{-T}^{T}\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x)-\Phi(x)\big|dx\bigg]>\frac{1}{2}\bigg(1-\sqrt{\frac{\alpha(1-\alpha)}{4}}\bigg)>0

for all
\delta\in[0,n^{-1/2})
and all
n\geq 16(C+T)^{2}
.

\bullet
Let us now consider the case
\delta\geq n^{-1/2}
and let

C\leq\big(\frac{1}{32\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}}\big)^{1/3}
. Following the idea of Chapter 2.6.1 in
Tsybakov (2009)
,
but with different hypotheses, we define
m\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\lfloor\frac{1}{4C}(n\delta^{2})^{1/3}\rfloor
,

h_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{T}{m}
and set

x_{k}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-T+2kh_{n},\quad k=0,\dots,m.

Further, we define

\displaystyle\varphi_{k,n}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}0\quad&x\in[x_{0},x_{k}]\\
\frac{\delta}{2}(x-x_{k})&x\in[x_{k},x_{k}+h_{n}],\\
\frac{\delta}{2}h_{n}+\delta(x-(x_{k}+h_{n}))&x\in[x_{k}+h_{n},x_{k+1}]\\
\frac{\delta}{2}h_{n}+\delta(x_{k+1}-(x_{k}+h_{n}))&x\in[x_{k+1},x_{m}]\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\psi_{k,n}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}0\quad&x\in[x_{0},x_{k}]\\
\delta(x-x_{k})\quad&x\in[x_{k},x_{k}+h_{n}],\\
\delta h_{n}+\frac{\delta}{2}(x-(x_{k}+h_{n}))&x\in[x_{k}+h_{n},x_{k+1}]\\
\delta h_{n}+\frac{\delta}{2}(x_{k+1}-(x_{k}+h_{n}))&x\in[x_{k+1},x_{m}]\end{cases}

for
k=0,\dots,m-1
. For
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{m})\in\{0,1\}^{m}
, we define

\Phi_{\gamma,n}\colon\mathbb{R}\to[0,1],\quad\Phi_{\gamma,n}|_{[-T,T]}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{4}+\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\gamma_{k+1}\varphi_{k,n}(x)+(1-\gamma_{k+1})\psi_{k,n}(x),

where the functions are defined outside
[-T,T]
by their values at the respective boundaries
and write
P_{\gamma,n}^{\otimes n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=P_{\Phi_{\gamma,n}}^{\otimes n}
. Obviously,

\Phi_{0,n},\Phi_{1,n}\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}
. A visualization of these hypotheses can be found in Figure
4
.
Note that for
n\geq 16T^{2}
, we have

n^{-1/2}\leq\frac{1}{4T}
and so for
\delta\leq\frac{1}{4T}
and all
x\in[-T,T]
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{\gamma,n}(x)

\displaystyle\geq\Phi_{\gamma,n}(-T)=\Phi_{\gamma,n}(x_{0})=1/4,

\displaystyle 1-\Phi_{\gamma,n}(x)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\Phi_{\gamma,n}(x_{m})\geq 3/4-2T\delta\geq 1/4.

Following Example 2.2 in
Tsybakov (2009)
, let

\rho(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\mathds{1}_{\{\gamma_{k}^{\prime}\neq\gamma_{k}\}}
, let

d_{k}(T_{n}^{\delta},\gamma_{k})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{x_{k}}^{x_{k+1}}|T_{n}^{\delta}(x)-\gamma_{k}\varphi_{k,n}(x)-(1-\gamma_{k})\psi_{k,n}(x)-1/4|dx

and note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\gamma,n}^{\otimes n}\bigg[\int_{-T}^{T}\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x)-\Phi_{\gamma,n}(x)\big|dx\bigg]=\mathbb{E}_{\gamma,n}^{\otimes n}\bigg[\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\int_{x_{k}}^{x_{k+1}}\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x)-\Phi_{\gamma,n}(x)\big|dx\bigg]=\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\mathbb{E}_{\gamma,n}^{\otimes n}\big[d_{k}(T_{n}^{\delta},\gamma_{k})\big].

Defining
\hat{\gamma}_{k}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{t=0,1}d_{k}(T_{n}^{\delta},t)
, we have

\displaystyle d_{k}(T_{n}^{\delta},\gamma_{k})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}d_{k}(\hat{\gamma}_{k}\varphi_{k,n}+(1-\hat{\gamma}_{k})\psi_{k,n}+1/4,\gamma_{k})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}|\hat{\gamma}_{k}-\gamma_{k}|\int_{x_{k}}^{x_{k+1}}|\varphi_{k,n}(x)-\psi_{k,n}(x)|dx

and so by noting that

\int_{x_{k}}^{x_{k+1}}|\varphi_{k,n}(x)-\psi_{k,n}(x)|dx=h_{n}|\varphi_{k,n}(x_{k}+h_{n})-\psi_{k,n}(x_{k}+h_{n})|\geq h_{n}2TC\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-1/3},

we obtain for all
\gamma\in\{0,1\}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\gamma,n}^{\otimes n}\bigg[\int_{-T}^{T}\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x)-\Phi_{\gamma,n}(x)\big|dx\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\mathbb{E}_{\gamma,n}^{\otimes n}\bigg[|\hat{\gamma}_{k}-\gamma_{k}|\int_{x_{k}}^{x_{k+1}}|\varphi_{k,n}(x)-\psi_{k,n}(x)|dx\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq h_{n}TC\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-1/3}\mathbb{E}_{\gamma,n}^{\otimes n}[\rho(\hat{\gamma},\gamma)].

Consequently, for any
T_{n}^{\delta}
,

\max_{\gamma\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\gamma,n}^{\otimes n}\bigg[\int_{-T}^{T}\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x)-\Phi_{\gamma,n}(x)\big|dx\bigg]\geq h_{n}TC\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-1/3}\inf_{\hat{\gamma}}\max_{\gamma\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\gamma,n}^{\otimes n}[\rho(\hat{\gamma},\gamma)].

By similar arguments as in the previous case, we have for
\alpha=64C^{3}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}
and all

\gamma^{\prime},\gamma\in\{0,1\}^{n}
with
\rho(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma)=1
,

\displaystyle h^{2}\big(P_{\gamma^{\prime},n}^{\otimes n},P_{\gamma,n}^{\otimes n}\big)

\displaystyle\leq n\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\int_{x_{k}}^{x_{k+1}}(\Phi_{\gamma^{\prime},n}(x)-\Phi_{\gamma,n}(x))^{2}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle=n\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}|\gamma_{k}^{\prime}-\gamma_{k}|\int_{x_{k}}^{x_{k+1}}(\varphi_{k,n}(x)-\psi_{k,n}(x))^{2}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\leq n\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}|\gamma_{k}^{\prime}-\gamma_{k}|\frac{1}{4}\int_{x_{k}}^{x_{k+1}}\delta^{2}h_{n}^{2}dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}n\delta^{2}h_{n}^{3}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}\rho(\gamma^{\prime},\gamma)

\displaystyle\leq n\delta^{2}(4C(n\delta^{2})^{-1/3})^{3}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}\leq 64C^{3}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}=\alpha<2.

Thus, by Theorem 2.12 (iii) of
Tsybakov (2009)
,

\inf_{\hat{\gamma}}\max_{\gamma\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\gamma}[\rho(\hat{\gamma},\gamma)]\geq\frac{m}{2}\big(1-\sqrt{\alpha(1-\alpha)/4}\big)=\frac{1}{2h_{n}}\big(1-\sqrt{\alpha(1-\alpha)/4}\big)

and so we have for any
T_{n}^{\delta}
,

\displaystyle\max_{\gamma\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\gamma,n}^{\otimes n}\bigg[\int_{-T}^{T}\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x)-\Phi_{\gamma,n}(x)\big|dx\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq h_{n}TC\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-1/3}\inf_{\hat{\gamma}}\max_{\gamma\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\gamma,n}^{\otimes n}[\rho(\hat{\gamma},\gamma)]

\displaystyle\geq\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{-1/3}\frac{1}{2}TC\big(1-\sqrt{\alpha(1-\alpha)/4}\big)>0

for all
\delta\in[n^{-1/2},\frac{1}{4T}]
and all
n\geq 16T^{2}
, implying

\inf_{T_{n}^{\delta}}\sup_{\Phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}}\Big(\sqrt{n}\wedge\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{1/3}\Big)\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\bigg[\int_{-T}^{T}\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x)-\Phi(x)\big|dx\bigg]>\frac{1}{2}TC\big(1-\sqrt{\alpha(1-\alpha)/4}\big)>0

for all
\delta\in[n^{-1/2},\frac{1}{4T}]
and all
n\geq 16T^{2}
.

In summary, we have shown for any
C\leq\min\big\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{8T}},\big(\frac{1}{32\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}}\big)^{1/3}\big\}
,

\displaystyle\inf_{T_{n}^{\delta}}\sup_{\Phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\delta}}\Big(\sqrt{n}\wedge\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)^{1/3}\Big)\mathbb{E}_{\Phi}^{\otimes n}\bigg[\int_{-T}^{T}\big|T_{n}^{\delta}(x)-\Phi(x)\big|dx\bigg]>\frac{1}{2}\big(1-\sqrt{\alpha(1-\alpha)/4}\big)\max\{TC,1\}>0

for
\alpha=\max\{16T^{2}C^{2},64C^{3}\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}\}
, all
\delta\in[0,\frac{1}{4T}]
and

n>\max\{12^{3}C^{3},16T^{2}\}
and so the assertion follows. ∎

C.2
Proof of Proposition
4.2

Proposition
4.2
is an immediate consequence of an application of Fubini’s theorem and the following Lemma,
which yields an upper bound on the convergence rate for
\mathbb{E}[|\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)-\Phi_{n}(t)|]
for all
t\in(-T,T)
. For
Proposition
4.2
, we utilize that in the subsequent result,
the maximum is equal to
(n/\delta_{n})^{-1/3}
if and only if

-T+(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}\leq t\leq T-(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}
.
Note that the following result is also used in the proof of Theorem
4.3
(i).

Lemma C.1
.

Assume
\Phi_{0}
to be continuously differentiable in a neighborhood of zero and let
\Phi_{0}^{\prime}(0)>0
. Then, for
n

large enough, there exists a constant
K>0
depending only on
\Phi_{0}
,
F_{X}
and the bounds on its derivatives, such
that for all
t\in(-T,T)
,

\mathbb{E}\big[|\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)-\Phi_{n}(t)|\big]\leq K\max\Big\{\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3},\big(n(T-t)\big)^{-1/2},\big(n(T+t)\big)^{-1/2}\Big\}.

Conceptually, the proof follows the idea of the proof of Theorem 1 in
Durot (2008)
. From a technical point of
view, however, the
n
-dependence of
\Phi_{n}
and its vanishing derivative required us to do some adjustments.

For ease of notation, let
x_{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\max\{x,0\}
denote the positive part of
x
for every
x\in\mathbb{R}
, let
K>0

denote a constant which may changes from line to line depending only on
\Phi_{0}^{\prime}
in a neighborhood of zero and on

p_{X}
and define

I_{1}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{E}\big[(\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)-\Phi_{n}(t))_{+}\big]\quad\text{and}\quad I_{2}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{E}\big[(\Phi_{n}(t)-\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t))_{+}\big]

for
t\in[-T,T]
, implying that

\mathbb{E}\big[|\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)-\Phi_{n}(t)|\big]=I_{1}(t)+I_{2}(t).

From
|\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)-\Phi_{n}(t)|\leq 1
, we obtain

I_{1}(t)=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{P}\big(\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)-\Phi_{n}(t)>x\big)dx

and from the fact that
\hat{\Phi}_{n}
maps into
[0,1]
, we observe

I_{1}(t)=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{P}\big(\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)>x+\Phi_{n}(t)\big)dx=\int_{0}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}\mathbb{P}\big(\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)>x+\Phi_{n}(t)\big)dx.

By the switch relation (Lemma
2.2
), this implies

I_{1}(t)=\int_{0}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}\hskip-1.99997pt\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<F_{n}^{-1}(F_{n}(t))\big)dx\leq\int_{0}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}\hskip-1.99997pt\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<t\big)dx.

Now note that for every
x>0
which satisfies
\Phi_{n}(t)+x<\Phi_{n}(T)
, a Taylor expansion with Lagrange remainder
of
\Phi_{n}^{-1}
around
\Phi_{n}(t)
yields for some
\nu_{n}\in(\Phi_{n}(t),\Phi_{n}(t)+x)
that

\Phi_{n}^{-1}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)=t+\frac{1}{\Phi_{n}^{\prime}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(\nu_{n}))}x\geq t+K\delta_{n}^{-1}x

for
n
large enough. By an addition of zero, by using that
t-\Phi_{n}^{-1}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<0
and by
Lemma
4.4
(ii), we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<t\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)-\Phi_{n}^{-1}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<t-\Phi_{n}^{-1}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(|F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)-\Phi_{n}^{-1}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)|\geq\Phi_{n}^{-1}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)-t\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(|F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)-\Phi_{n}^{-1}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)|\geq K\delta_{n}^{-1}x\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathds{1}_{\{x<K(\delta_{n}/n)^{1/3}\}}+\mathds{1}_{\{x\geq K(\delta_{n}/n)^{1/3}\}}K\delta_{n}n^{-1}x^{-3}.

Because we assumed
x<\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)
, we now have

\displaystyle I_{1}(t)\leq K\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)^{1/3}

\displaystyle+K\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{-3}\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[K(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n})^{1/3},\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)]\}}dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<t\big)dx.

Note that
\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)=\mathcal{O}(\delta_{n})
and so we can choose
n
large enough, such that

\displaystyle K\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{-3}\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[K(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n})^{1/3},\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)]\}}dx

\displaystyle\leq K\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)^{1/3}

and consequently,

I_{1}(t)\leq K\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)^{1/3}+\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<t\big)dx.

To derive an upper bound for the remaining integral, note that for every
x\geq\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)
, we have

\Phi_{n}^{-1}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)=T
. So again by Lemma
4.4
(ii) and for
n
large enough,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<t\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)-T<t-T\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(|F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)-T|\geq T-t\big)

\displaystyle\leq K(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1}(T-t)^{-3}

for
1-\Phi_{n}(t)>x\geq\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)
.

To finish the proof, we will consider the cases
T-t\geq(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}
and
T-t\leq(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}

separately.

\bullet
Let us start by assuming
T-t\geq(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}
. Then,

\displaystyle\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<t\big)dx

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)}^{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)+(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n})^{1/3}}K(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1}(T-t)^{-3}dx

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)+(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n})^{1/3}}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<t\big)dx,

where

\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)}^{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)+(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n})^{1/3}}K(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1}(T-t)^{-3}dx=K\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)^{1/3}(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1}(T-t)^{-3}\leq K\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)^{1/3}

and by Lemma
E.1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)+(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n})^{1/3}}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<t\big)dx

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)+(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n})^{1/3}}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}\mathbb{P}\big(|F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)-T|\geq T-t\big)dx

\displaystyle\qquad\leq K\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)+(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n})^{1/3}}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}(n(T-t))^{-1}(\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)-x)^{-2}dx

\displaystyle\qquad=K(n(T-t))^{-1}\big[(\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)-x)^{-1}\big]_{x=\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)+(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n})^{1/3}}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}

\displaystyle\qquad=K(n(T-t))^{-1}\Big((1-\Phi_{n}(T))^{-1}+\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq K\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)^{2/3}+K\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)^{1/3}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq K\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)^{1/3}.

So we have shown

I_{1}(t)\leq K\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)^{1/3}

for
T-t\geq(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}
.

\bullet
Now assume
T-t\leq(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}
. Then,

(n(T-t))^{-1/2}\geq\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{n}\Big)^{1/3}

and we have

\displaystyle I_{1}(t)\leq K(n(T-t))^{-1/2}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)}^{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)+(n(T-t))^{-1/2}}1dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)+(n(T-t))^{-1/2}}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<t\big)dx.

As before, we know from Lemma
E.1
that

\displaystyle\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)+(n(T-t))^{-1/2}}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}\mathbb{P}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(t)+x)<t\big)dx

\displaystyle\qquad\leq K(n(T-t))^{-1}\big[(\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)-x)^{-1}\big]_{x=\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)+(n(T-t))^{-1/2}}^{1-\Phi_{n}(t)}

\displaystyle\qquad=K(n(T-t))^{-1}\big((1-\Phi_{n}(T))^{-1}+(n(T-t))^{1/2}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq K(n(T-t))^{-1}+K(n(T-t))^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq K(n(T-t))^{-1/2}

and so we have shown

I_{1}(t)\leq K(n(T-t))^{-1/2}

for
T-t\leq(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}
.

Summarizing the results, we have

I_{1}(t)\leq K\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}+(n(T-t))^{-1/2}\Big)

and by similar arguments,

I_{2}(t)\leq K\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}+(n(T+t))^{-1/2}\Big).

Thus,

\mathbb{E}\big[|\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)-\Phi_{n}(t)|\big]\leq K\max\Big\{\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3},(n(T-t))^{-1/2},(n(T+t))^{-1/2}\Big\}

for
n
large enough which proves the assertion.
∎

C.3
Proof of Theorem
4.3
(i)

The concept of proof presented below, namely to employ the switch relation to move over from
\hat{\Phi}_{n}
and
\Phi_{n}
to their inverse counterparts and to analyze the

L^{1}
-limit of these inverse counterparts, appeared first in

Groeneboom (1985)
, was made rigorous in Corollary 2.1 of
Groeneboom et al. (1999)
and was later considerably generalized in
Durot (2007)
and
Durot (2008)
.

Further notation.
Throughout this section, we use the notation introduced in Section
4
and
recall
\lambda_{n}=\Phi_{n}\circ F_{X}^{-1}
. Next, we assume
\tilde{U}_{n}
on
[0,1]
and
\Phi_{n}^{-1}
,
\lambda_{n}^{-1}
on
[\Phi_{n}(-T),\Phi_{n}(T)]

to be continuously extended to functions on the real line by their values at the respective boundary points of their original domains.
Note that this extension satisfies
\lambda_{n}^{-1}=F_{X}\circ\Phi_{n}^{-1}
. Finally, we abbreviate

\sigma_{n}^{2}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Phi_{n}(t)(1-\Phi_{n}(t)),\quad\Lambda_{n}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{n}(u)du\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{J}_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{-T}^{T}|\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)-\Phi_{n}(t)|dt

for
t\in[-T,T]
and
s\in[0,1]
. Recall that throughout,

P_{X}
is compactly supported on

\mathcal{X}=[-T,T]
for some
T>0
with continuous, strictly positive Lebesgue density
p_{X}
on
\mathcal{X}
.

The proof is subdivided into six claims. Right before stating a claim, additional notation will be introduced if required.
Throughout the proof,
K
denotes a universal constant which may changes from line to line.

Claim I:

\mathcal{J}_{n}=\mathcal{J}_{n,1}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
with
\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{n,1}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{\Phi_{n}(-T)}^{\Phi_{n}(T)}|F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)|da
.

Note that here, as compared to the classical asymptotics, the integration domain is

n
-dependent with length of order
\delta_{n}
.

Proof of Claim I.

The subsequent proof is based on the tail bound of the inverse process given in
Lemma
4.4
(ii). In that way, the proof hinges on the convergence rate of the inverse process, which is
again highlighted by the necessary localizations of the integration domain.
Let

I_{1}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{-T}^{T}\big(\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)-\Phi_{n}(t)\big)_{+}dt
,

I_{2}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{-T}^{T}\big(\Phi_{n}(t)-\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)\big)_{+}dt
and

J_{1}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{0}^{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)}\mathds{1}_{\{\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)>\Phi_{n}(t)+u\}}du\,dt.

By Cavalieri’s principle applied to
I_{1}
,

\displaystyle I_{1}-J_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\mathds{1}_{\{\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)>\Phi_{n}(t)+u\}}du\,dt-\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{0}^{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)}\mathds{1}_{\{\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)>\Phi_{n}(t)+u\}}du\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{-T}^{T}\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)}^{1}\mathds{1}_{\{\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)>\Phi_{n}(t)+u\}}du\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(T))}^{T}\int_{\Phi_{n}(T)-\Phi_{n}(t)}^{1}\mathds{1}_{\{\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)>\Phi_{n}(t)+u\}}du\,dt,

where the last equality is based on
\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)>\Phi_{n}(T)

if and only if
F_{n}^{-1}(F_{n}(t))>F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(T))
by the switch relation
(Lemma
2.2
). Thus,
I_{1}-J_{1}\geq 0
and again by Cavalieri’s principle,

\displaystyle I_{1}-J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(T))}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\mathds{1}_{\{\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)>\Phi_{n}(t)+u\}}du\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{T-(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}^{T}|\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)-\Phi_{n}(t)|dt+2T\mathds{1}_{\big\{F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(T))\leq T-\frac{\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}{(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/3}}\big\}}

where we used without loss of generality that
n\delta_{n}^{2}\geq 1
for
n
large enough. For
\varepsilon>0
,
Lemma
4.4
(ii) provides for
n
large enough,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sqrt{n}\mathds{1}_{\big\{F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(T))\leq T-\frac{\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}{(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/3}}\big\}}\geq\varepsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(|F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(\Phi_{n}(T))-T|\geq\frac{\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}{(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/3}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq K\big(n\delta_{n}^{2}\big((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})\big)^{3}\big)^{-1}

which is bounded by
K\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-3}
and so we have

I_{1}-J_{1}\leq\int_{T-(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}^{T}|\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)-\Phi_{n}(t)|dt+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

By Markov’s inequality, Fubini’s theorem and Proposition
C.1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sqrt{n}\int_{T-(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}^{T}|\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)-\Phi_{n}(t)|dt>\varepsilon\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq K\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\varepsilon}\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}\big(\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})-1\big)+K\frac{\sqrt{n}}{\varepsilon}\int_{T-(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}}^{T}n^{-1/2}(T-t)^{-1/2}dt

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{K}{\varepsilon}(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}\big(1+\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})\big)

and so we have shown that
I_{1}=J_{1}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
. Note further that by the change of variable

a=\Phi_{n}(t)+u
, Fubini’s theorem and the switch relation (Lemma
2.2
),

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Phi_{n}(-T)}^{\Phi_{n}(T)}\int_{-T}^{T}\mathds{1}_{\{a\geq\Phi_{n}(t)\}}\mathds{1}_{\{\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)>a\}}dt\,da

\displaystyle=\int_{\Phi_{n}(-T)}^{\Phi_{n}(T)}\int_{F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)}^{T}\mathds{1}_{\{\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)\geq t\}}\mathds{1}_{\{\hat{\Phi}_{n}(t)>a\}}dt\,da

\displaystyle=\int_{\Phi_{n}(-T)}^{\Phi_{n}(T)}\int_{F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)}^{\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)}\mathds{1}_{\{F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)<t\}}dt\,da

\displaystyle=\int_{\Phi_{n}(-T)}^{\Phi_{n}(T)}\big(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)-F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)\big)\mathds{1}_{\{F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)<\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)\}}da

and so we have

I_{1}=\int_{\Phi_{n}(-T)}^{\Phi_{n}(T)}\big(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)-F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)\big)\mathds{1}_{\{F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)<\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)\}}da+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

By similar arguments,

I_{2}=\int_{\Phi_{n}(-T)}^{\Phi_{n}(T)}\big(F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)\big)\mathds{1}_{\{F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)\geq\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)\}}da+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})

and Claim I follows.

Claim II:
There exist Brownian bridges
B_{n}
on
[0,1]
, such that
\mathcal{J}_{n,1}=\mathcal{J}_{n,2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
,
with

\mathcal{J}_{n,2}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{\lambda_{n}(0)}^{\lambda_{n}(1)}\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da.

Proof of Claim II.
Note first that
\Phi_{n}(T)=\lambda_{n}(1)
and

\Phi_{n}(-T)=\lambda_{n}(0)
by definition and that by Theorem 3 of
Komlós et al. (1975)
, there
exist Brownian bridges
B_{n}
on
[0,1]
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\bigg|F_{n}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(t)-t-\frac{B_{n}(t)}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|^{r}\bigg]^{1/r}=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{\log(n)}{n}\Big)

(C.1)

for
r\geq 1
. By definition of
\mathcal{J}_{n,1}
and rewriting
\Phi_{n}^{-1}=F_{X}^{-1}\circ\lambda_{n}^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{n,1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\lambda_{n}(0)}^{\lambda_{n}(1)}\bigg|F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-F_{X}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)+\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+F_{X}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-F_{X}^{-1}\circ\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|da.

A Taylor expansion of
F_{X}^{-1}
around
\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)
yields

F_{X}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-F_{X}^{-1}\circ\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)=\frac{\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}+\frac{1}{2}(F_{X}^{-1})^{\prime\prime}(\nu_{n})\big(\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big)^{2}

for some
\nu_{n}
between
\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)
and
\tilde{U}_{n}(a)
. But

(F_{X}^{-1})^{\prime\prime}=-\frac{p_{X}^{\prime}\circ F_{X}^{-1}}{(p_{X}\circ F_{X}^{-1})^{3}}
is bounded as
p_{X}
is continuously
differentiable and
p_{X}
is bounded away from zero, whence

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|F_{X}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-F_{X}^{-1}\circ\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-\frac{\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|\bigg]\leq K(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-2/3}

by Corollary
4.5
for
Z_{1,n}=Z_{2,n}=0
.
Combined with
|\lambda_{n}(1)-\lambda_{n}(0)|=\mathcal{O}(\delta_{n})
and

n\delta_{n}^{2}\longrightarrow\infty
, an application of Markov’s inequality
and Fubini’s theorem imply

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{n}

\displaystyle=\int_{\lambda_{n}(0)}^{\lambda_{n}(1)}\bigg|F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-F_{X}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)+\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\bigg(\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg)\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|da+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Now before we bring the KMT approximation (
C.1
) into play, we need to approximate the difference

F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-F_{X}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)
within the integral appropriately. To make this precise, observe

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{n}(0)}^{\lambda_{n}(1)}\bigg|F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-F_{X}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)+\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|da

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq K\delta_{n}\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\bigg|F_{n}^{-1}(u)-F_{X}^{-1}(u)+\frac{B_{n}(F_{X}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(u))}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}(F_{n}^{-1}(u))}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\lambda_{n}(0)}^{\lambda_{n}(1)}\bigg|\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}-\frac{B_{n}(F_{X}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{U}_{n}(a)))}{p_{X}(F_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{U}_{n}(a)))}\bigg|da

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=K\delta_{n}\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\bigg|F_{X}^{-1}(u)-F_{n}^{-1}(u)-\frac{B_{n}(F_{X}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(u))}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}(F_{n}^{-1}(u))}\bigg|+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}),

where the last equality follows from Lemma
4.4
(ii) and the classical bound on the expected modulus
of continuity of the Brownian bridge (e.g. formula (2) in
Fischer and Nappo (2010)
, rewriting the Brownian bridge in terms of an independent standard Gaussian random variable and a Brownian motion). Now by decomposing

\sup_{u\in[0,1]}=\max_{i}\sup_{u\in[i/n,(i+1)/n)}
and by utilizing that we can write

\max_{i}\sup_{u\in[i/n,(i+1)/n)}|F_{X}^{-1}(u)-F_{X}^{-1}(i/n)|=\mathcal{O}(n^{-1})
, we find

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\bigg|F_{X}^{-1}(u)-F_{n}^{-1}(u)-\frac{B_{n}(F_{X}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(u))}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}(F_{n}^{-1}(u))}\bigg|

\displaystyle\ \leq\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\bigg|F_{X}^{-1}(F_{n}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(u))-F_{X}^{-1}(u)-\frac{B_{n}(u)}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}(F_{X}^{-1}(u))}\bigg|+\mathcal{O}(n^{-1})

\displaystyle\ \leq\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\bigg|(F_{X}^{-1})^{\prime}(u)(F_{n}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(u)-u)-\frac{B_{n}(u)}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}(F_{X}^{-1}(u))}\bigg|+\mathcal{O}(n^{-1})+K\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\big|(F_{n}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(u)-u)^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\ \leq\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\bigg|F_{n}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(u)-u-\frac{B_{n}(u)}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(F_{X}^{-1}(u))}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1})=o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})

by (
C.1
) and the fact that

\sup_{u\in[0,1]}|F_{n}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(u)-u|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
.

The punch line of the next claim is to incorporate the Brownian bridges
B_{n}
from
Claim II into the inverse process.

Further notation.
For
a\in[\lambda_{n}(0),\lambda_{n}(1)]
, let
i_{n}(a)
denote the integer part of the expression

(a-\lambda_{n}(0))(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/3}/(\delta_{n}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2}))
, define

a_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\lambda_{n}(0)+i_{n}(a)\delta_{n}(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})
and

a_{n}^{B}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=a-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}))}{n^{1/2}(\lambda_{n}^{-1})^{\prime}(a_{n})}.

(C.2)

For
i\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, let
k_{i}^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\lambda_{n}(0)+i\delta_{n}(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})
,

I_{i}^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\big[k_{i}^{n},\min\{k_{i+1}^{n},\lambda_{n}(1)\}\big),

let
N^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\lambda_{n}(1)-\lambda_{n}(0))\frac{(n\delta_{n}^{2})}{\delta_{n}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}
and note that

\bigcup_{i=0}^{N^{n}}I_{i}^{n}=[\lambda_{n}(0),\lambda_{n}(1)]
.
Let us also define the interval
J_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{N^{n}-2}I_{i}^{n}
. The definition of the intervals and the
behavior of
a_{n}^{B}
is visualized in Figure
8
.

\lambda_{n}(0)
\lambda_{n}(1)
k_{1}^{n}
k_{2}^{n}
k_{3}^{n}
k_{N^{n}-2}^{n}
k_{N^{n}-1}^{n}
k_{N^{n}}^{n}
I_{0}^{n}
I_{1}^{n}
I_{2}^{n}
I_{N^{n}-2}^{n}
I_{N^{n}-1}^{n}
I_{N^{n}}^{n}
a
a_{n}^{B}
\delta_{n}(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})
2C\delta_{n}n^{-1/2}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})

Figure 8:
For
a\in I_{i}^{n}
for some
i\in\{0,\dots,N^{n}\}
, we have
|a_{n}^{B}-a|\leq C\delta_{n}(\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})/n)^{1/2}

for
n
large enough, which is smaller than the length of an interval
I_{i}^{n}
, bounded by

\delta_{n}(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})
.

Claim III:

\mathcal{J}_{n,2}=\mathcal{J}_{n,3}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
, with

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{n,3}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{\lambda_{n}(0)}^{\lambda_{n}(1)}\frac{|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da.

Proof of Claim III.
Let

\Omega_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\big\{\sup_{u\in[0,1]}|B_{n}(u)|\leq\sqrt{\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}\big\}\subset\Omega
and note that

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{n})\longrightarrow 1
as
n\longrightarrow\infty
. Then,

\mathcal{J}_{n,2}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}=\int_{J_{n}}\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}da+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}),

where we used Corollary
4.5
with
c_{n}=0
. Note further that
i_{n}(a)=i
for every

a\in I_{i}^{n}
and so in this case,

a_{n}=\lambda_{n}(0)+i\delta_{n}(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})=k_{i}^{n}

on
I_{i}^{n}
. Thus, for
a\in I_{i}^{n}
,
a_{n}^{B}
is just a translation of
a
by

B_{i}^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(k_{i}^{n}))}{\sqrt{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1})^{\prime}(k_{i}^{n})}.

Let
I_{i}^{n,B}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=I_{i}^{n}+B_{i}^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{x+B_{i}^{n}\mid x\in I_{i}^{n}\}
.
Then, a change of variable
inside the integral, where
a
is replaced by
a_{n}^{B}
on each interval
I_{i}^{n}
, proves that

\mathcal{J}_{n,2}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}
is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N^{n}-2}\hskip-2.84526pt\int_{I_{i}^{n}}\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}da+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=\hskip-2.84526pt\sum_{i=1}^{N^{n}-2}\hskip-2.84526pt\int_{I_{i}^{n,B}}\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B})-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}da+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=\hskip-2.84526pt\sum_{i=1}^{N^{n}-2}\hskip-2.84526pt\int_{I_{i}^{n}}\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B})-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}da+R_{n}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}),

where, with
I_{i}^{n,B}\triangle I_{i}^{n}
denoting the symmetric difference of the sets
I_{i}^{n,B}
and
I_{i}^{n}
,

\displaystyle|R_{n}|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N^{n}-2}\int_{I_{i}^{n,B}\triangle I_{i}^{n}}\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B})-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}da.

(C.3)

By definition of
\Omega_{n}
, we have
|a_{n}^{B}-a|\leq C\delta_{n}\sqrt{\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})/n}
on
\Omega_{n}
for
some constant
C>0
that does not depend on
a\in[\lambda_{n}(0),\lambda_{n}(1)]
and thus on
\Omega_{n}
,
a_{n}^{B}
is in
fact contained in
[\lambda_{n}(0),\lambda_{n}(1)]
for
a\in J_{n}
and
n
large enough. Let

D_{i}^{n}
denote the symmetric difference of
I_{i}^{n}
and the union

(I_{i}^{n}+C\delta_{n}(\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})/n)^{1/2})\cup(I_{i}^{n}-C\delta_{n}(\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})/n)^{1/2})
.
Then,
I_{i}^{n,B}\triangle I_{i}^{n}\subset D_{i}^{n}
on
\Omega_{n}
and we obtain with (
C.3
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|R_{n}|]

\displaystyle\leq K\sum_{i=1}^{N^{n}-2}\int_{D_{i}^{n}}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B})\big|\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}\big]+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}\bigg]da

\displaystyle\leq K(\lambda_{n}(1)-\lambda_{n}(0))\frac{(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/3}}{\delta_{n}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}\big((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}+n^{-1/2}\big)\delta_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}{n}}\leq K\delta_{n}\frac{n^{-1/2}}{\sqrt{\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}},

where we used Corollary
4.5
with

c_{n}=\delta_{n}\sqrt{\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})/n}
. Thus,

\mathcal{J}_{n,2}=\int_{J_{n}}\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B})-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}da+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Subsequently, we show that we can replace
a_{n}^{B}
by
a
in the argument of the Brownian bridge and the density

p_{X}
in the previous expression. By a Taylor expansion of
1/p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))
around
a
, we find for
some
\nu_{n}
between
a
and
a_{n}^{B}
, that

\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}=\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}+\frac{p_{X}^{\prime}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(\nu_{n}))}{(p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(\nu_{n}))^{2}\Phi_{0}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}\Phi_{n}^{-1}(\nu_{n}))}\frac{(a_{n}^{B}-a)}{\delta_{n}}.

Similar as before and by the same application of Corollary
4.5
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{J_{n}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B})-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\bigg(\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}-\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg)\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}\bigg]da\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq K(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}\big(\lambda_{n}(1)-\lambda_{n}(0)\big)n^{-1/2}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2}),

which shows that

\mathcal{J}_{n,2}=\int_{J_{n}}\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B})-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

Next, we observe

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{J_{n}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B})-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B})-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\bigg)\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}\bigg]da\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq Kn^{-1/2}\int_{J_{n}}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B}))\big|\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}\big]da

and obtain by the classical bound on the expected modulus of continuity of the Brownian bridge,

\mathcal{J}_{n,2}=\int_{J_{n}}\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B})-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

To complete the proof of Claim III, it is sufficient to verify that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\lambda_{n}(0)-\lambda_{n}(1))\sup_{a\in J_{n}}\bigg|\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B})+\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}))}{\sqrt{n}}-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\bigg|\longrightarrow_{\mathbb{P}}0

(C.4)

as
n\longrightarrow\infty
. A Taylor expansion of
\lambda_{n}^{-1}
around
a\in J_{n}
reveals for some

\nu_{n}=\nu_{n}(a,a_{n}^{B})
between
a_{n}^{B}
and
a
the identity

\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}^{B})+\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}))}{\sqrt{n}}-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)=\bigg(1-\frac{(\lambda_{n}^{-1})^{\prime}(\nu_{n})}{(\lambda_{n}^{-1})^{\prime}(a_{n})}\bigg)\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}))}{\sqrt{n}}.

Evaluation of the right-hand side on
\Omega_{n}
in terms of
\Phi_{0}
,
\delta_{n}
and
p_{X}
together with

\sup_{a\in J_{n}}|\nu_{n}(a,a_{n}^{B})-a_{n}|\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}\leq\sup_{a\in J_{n}}|a_{n}^{B}-a|\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}\leq K\delta_{n}(\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})/n)^{1/2}

finally yields (
C.4
) and Claim III follows.

Further notation:
Let
\displaystyle L_{n}\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{R},L_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{n}^{2}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(u)du
and define

U_{n}^{L}\colon[\lambda_{n}(0),\lambda_{n}(1)]\to[0,1],\quad U_{n}^{L}(a)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=L_{n}\big(\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})\big)-L_{n}\big(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big).

Claim IV:

\mathcal{J}_{n,3}=\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{n}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
, with

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{J_{n}}\bigg|\frac{U_{n}^{L}(a)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da.

Proof of Claim IV.
It suffices to show that

\int_{J_{n}}\bigg(\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big|-\bigg|\frac{U_{n}^{L}(a)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|\bigg)\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da=o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2}).

As in the previous claim, we argue on
\Omega_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\big\{\sup_{u\in[0,1]}|B_{n}(u)|\leq\sqrt{\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}\big\}\subset\Omega
. A Taylor expansion of
L_{n}
around
\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)
provides the equality

U_{n}^{L}(a)=L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\big(\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big)+\frac{1}{2}L_{n}^{\prime\prime}(\nu_{n})\big(\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big)^{2}

for some
\nu_{n}
between
\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)
and
\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})
. Recalling the definition

\sigma_{n}^{2}(t)=\Phi_{n}(t)(1-\Phi_{n}(t))
,

L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))=\Phi_{n}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))\big(1-\Phi_{n}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))\big)\geq\Phi_{n}(-T)(1-\Phi_{n}(T))\geq K

for all
a\in[\lambda_{n}(0),\lambda_{n}(1)]
, while

|L_{n}^{\prime\prime}(\nu_{n})|=\big|(\sigma_{n}^{2}\circ F_{X}^{-1})^{\prime}(\nu_{n})\big|=\bigg|\Phi_{n}^{\prime}(F_{X}^{-1}(\nu_{n}))\frac{1-2\Phi_{n}(F_{X}^{-1}(\nu_{n}))}{p_{X}(F_{X}^{-1}(\nu_{n}))}\bigg|\leq\delta_{n}K.

Thus, by the reverse triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\bigg|\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})

\displaystyle-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big|-\bigg|\frac{U_{n}^{L}(a)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|\bigg|\leq\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-\frac{U_{n}^{L}(a)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-\big(\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{L_{n}^{\prime\prime}(\nu_{n})}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\big(\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big)^{2}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}K\big(\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big)^{2}

for all
a\in J_{n}
. Consequently,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big|-\bigg|\frac{U_{n}^{L}(a)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|\bigg|\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}\bigg]\leq\delta_{n}K\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big)^{2}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}}\Big],

which is bounded by
K(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-2/3}\delta_{n}
by Corollary
4.5
, applied with

c_{n}=\delta_{n}(\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})/n)^{1/2}
and
Z_{2,n}=0
.
Markov’s inequality, Fubini’s theorem and
\lambda_{n}(1)-\lambda_{n}(0)=\mathcal{O}(\delta_{n})
then reveal
for any
\varepsilon>0
the bound

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sqrt{n}\int_{J_{n}}\hskip-4.2679pt\bigg(\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big|-\bigg|\frac{U_{n}^{L}(a)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|\bigg)\frac{da}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}>\varepsilon,\,\Omega_{n}\bigg)\leq\frac{K\delta_{n}}{\varepsilon(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}}.

The goal of the next claim is to bring another strong Gaussian approximation into play, namely standard Brownian
motions
W_{n}
given
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
, conditionally independent of the Brownian bridges
B_{n}
of the KMT approximation,
that satisfy for some constant
A>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\bigg|\Upsilon_{n}(t)-\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{n}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(u)du-\frac{W_{n}(L^{n}(t))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|^{q}\bigg|X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\bigg]\leq An^{1-q}.

(C.5)

Noting that
Y_{i}^{n}-\Phi_{n}(X_{i})
,
i=1,\dots,n
, are bounded and conditionally centered and independent given
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
, existence of such
W_{n}
’s is guaranteed by
Sakhanenko (1985)
.

Further notation:
Define

L^{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{n}^{2}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(u)du,\qquad\psi_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{L_{n}^{\prime\prime}(t)}{\sqrt{n}L_{n}^{\prime}(t)}B_{n}(t),\qquad d_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sqrt{\delta_{n}}\frac{|\lambda_{n}^{\prime}(t)|}{2L_{n}^{\prime}(t)^{2}}

for
t\in[0,1]
and let
\mathbb{P}^{|X}
denote the conditional measure given
(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})
.
For

n
large enough to ensure the subsequent expression being well-defined for
|u|\leq(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}})^{1/3}L^{n}(t)

and any
t\in(0,1)
, define for the
\mathbb{P}^{|X}
-Brownian motions
W_{n}
fulfilling (
C.5
),

W_{t}^{n}(u)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\psi_{n}(t)}}\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/6}\Big(W_{n}\Big(L^{n}(t)+\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}u(1-\psi_{n}(t))\Big)-W_{n}\big(L^{n}(t)\big)\Big).

Note that
W_{t}^{n}
is therefore
distributed as a standard two-sided Brownian motion under
\mathbb{P}^{|X}
for every
t\in(0,1)
. In addition, define

\tilde{V}_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{|u|\leq\delta_{n}^{-1}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}\{W_{t}^{n}(u)+d_{n}(t)u^{2}\}

and set

\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{n,1}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\int_{0}^{1}|\tilde{V}_{n}(t)|\frac{|\Phi_{n}^{\prime}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(t)|}{(p_{X}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(t))^{2}|L_{n}^{\prime}(t)|}dt.

Claim V:
For the
\mathbb{P}^{|X}
-standard Brownian motions
W_{n}
from (
C.5
), the distribution of both
(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{n,1}-\mu_{n})
and

(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}((\frac{n}{\delta_{n}})^{1/3}\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{n}-\mu_{n})
with respect to
\mathbb{P}^{|X}
have the same weak limit in probability.

Proof of Claim V.

Let us define

\displaystyle T_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\delta_{n}^{-1}(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{\frac{1}{3(3q-5)}}

(C.6)

for some
q\geq 12
and let
\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\subset\Omega
denote the measurable set on which the following inequalities hold

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in[0,1]}

\displaystyle|B_{n}(u)|\leq\log(n\delta_{n}^{2}),\quad\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\bigg|F_{X}^{-1}(u)-F_{n}^{-1}(u)-\frac{B_{n}(F_{X}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(u))}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}(F_{n}^{-1}(u))}\bigg|\leq\frac{\log(n)^{2}}{n},

\displaystyle\sup_{|u-v|\leq T_{n}(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}})^{-1/3}\sqrt{\log(n)}}|B_{n}(u)-B_{n}(v)|\leq\sqrt{T_{n}}\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/6}\log(n),

where
B_{n}
denote the Brownian bridges from Claim II. Note that

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{n}^{\prime})\longrightarrow 1
for
n\longrightarrow\infty
, so w.l.o.g. we prove the
claim on
\Omega_{n}^{\prime}
. For readability, the proof is divided into multiple steps.

\bullet
For every
a\in J_{n}
, let us introduce

I_{n}(a)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Big[\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}\big(L_{n}(0)-L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\big),\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}\big(L_{n}(1)-L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\big)\Big],

which is the subset over which the
\operatorname*{argmin}
in the definition of
U_{n}^{L}(a)
is considered, as shown subsequently.
By using elementary properties of the
\operatorname*{argmin}
,

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}U_{n}^{L}(a)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}\bigg(L_{n}\bigg(\operatorname*{argmin}_{u\in[0,1]}\{\Upsilon_{n}(u)-a_{n}^{B}u\}\bigg)-L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\bigg)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}\bigg(\operatorname*{argmin}_{v\in[L_{n}(0),L_{n}(1)]}\big\{\big(\Upsilon_{n}\circ L_{n}^{-1}-a_{n}^{B}L_{n}^{-1}\big)(v)\big\}-L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\bigg)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}\hskip-5.69054pt\operatorname*{argmin}_{v:\,v+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\in[L_{n}(0),L_{n}(1)]}\big\{\big(\Upsilon_{n}\circ L_{n}^{-1}-a_{n}^{B}L_{n}^{-1}\big)\big(v+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\big)\big\}

\displaystyle=\operatorname*{argmin}_{v\in I_{n}(a)}\Big\{\big(\Upsilon_{n}\circ L_{n}^{-1}-a_{n}^{B}L_{n}^{-1}\big)\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}v+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle=\operatorname*{argmin}_{v\in I_{n}(a)}\bigg\{\frac{n^{2/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}\big(\Upsilon_{n}\circ L_{n}^{-1}-a_{n}^{B}L_{n}^{-1}\big)\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}v+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{n^{2/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}\big(\Lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-a\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big)-\frac{n^{2/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}(a-a_{n}^{B})\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\bigg\}.

Defining further for
a\in J_{n}
and
u\in I_{n}(a)
,

\displaystyle D_{n}(a,u)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{n^{2/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}\big(\Lambda_{n}\circ L_{n}^{-1}-aL_{n}^{-1}\big)\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}u+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\Big)-\frac{n^{2/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}\big(\Lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-a\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big),

\displaystyle R_{n}(a,u)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{n^{2/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}\int_{\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}^{L_{n}^{-1}((\frac{n}{\delta_{n}})^{-1/3}u+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)))}\Phi_{n}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(x)-\Phi_{n}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(x)dx

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n^{2/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}(a-a_{n}^{B})\Big(L_{n}^{-1}\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}u+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\Big)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\Big),

\displaystyle\tilde{R}_{n}(a,u)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{n^{2/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}\Upsilon_{n}\circ L_{n}^{-1}\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}u+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{n^{2/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}\int_{0}^{L_{n}^{-1}((\frac{n}{\delta_{n}})^{-1/3}u+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)))}\hskip-3.99994pt\Phi_{n}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(x)dx-W_{\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}^{n}(u)-\frac{n^{1/6}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}W_{n}(L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))),

we see for every
a\in J_{n}
,

\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}U_{n}^{L}(a)=\operatorname*{argmin}_{u\in I_{n}(a)}\big\{D_{n}(a,u)+W_{\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}^{n}(u)+R_{n}(a,u)+\tilde{R}_{n}(a,u)\big\},

where the expressions in the
\operatorname*{argmin}
on the right-hand side deviate from the expressions in the
\operatorname*{argmin}
on the
left-hand side only by a term which does not depend on
u
.

\bullet
Before we show in the next step that both,
R_{n}
and
\tilde{R}_{n}
, are negligible for the
location of the
\operatorname*{argmin}
, we localize. So let

\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{|u|\leq T_{n}}\big\{D_{n}(a,u)+W_{\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}^{n}(u)+R_{n}(a,u)+\tilde{R}_{n}(a,u)\big\}

for
a\in J_{n}
and note that
[-T_{n},T_{n}]\subset I_{n}(a)
at least for
n
large
enough, with
T_{n}
defined in (
C.6
). This follows from

T_{n}=n^{\frac{1}{3(3q-5)}}\delta_{n}^{-\frac{(9q-17)}{(9q-15)}}=n^{\frac{1}{3(3q-5)}}\delta_{n}^{-\frac{1}{3(3q-5)}}\delta_{n}^{-\frac{(3q-6)}{(3q-5)}}=\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3(3q-5)}}\delta_{n}^{-\frac{(3q-6)}{(3q-5)}}

and

\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}T_{n}=\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-\frac{(3q-6)}{3(3q-5)}}\delta_{n}^{-\frac{(3q-6)}{(3q-5)}}=(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-\frac{(3q-6)}{3(3q-5)}}=(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-\frac{q-2}{3q-5}}.

(C.7)

Note further that
(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}})^{1/3}U_{n}^{L}(a)

differs from
\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)
if and only if
(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}})^{1/3}|U_{n}^{L}(a)|>T_{n}
. But then, by a Taylor expansion
of
L_{n}
around
\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)
, Corollary
4.5
and by definition of
T_{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}U_{n}^{L}(a)\neq\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a),\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(|L_{n}(\tilde{U}_{n}(a_{n}^{B}))-L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))|>T_{n}\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3},\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq K(\delta_{n}T_{n})^{-3q/2}=K(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-\frac{q}{2(3q-5)}}.

Using this inequality, we have for any
\varepsilon>0
and
n
large enough, by Markov’s inequality, Fubini’s
theorem, Hölder’s inequality and Minkowski’s inequality, that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\bigg|\int_{J_{n}}\bigg(\bigg|\frac{(n/\delta_{n})^{1/3}U_{n}^{L}(a)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|-\bigg|\frac{\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|\bigg)\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da\bigg|>\varepsilon,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}}{\varepsilon}\int_{J_{n}}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathds{1}_{\{(n/\delta_{n})^{1/3}U_{n}^{L}(a)\neq\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)\}}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\big]^{(r-1)/r}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\frac{(n/\delta_{n})^{1/3}U_{n}^{L}(a)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|^{r}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]^{1/r}+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg|\frac{\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|^{r}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]^{1/r}\bigg)\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da,

which is bounded by
\frac{K}{\varepsilon}(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-\frac{q(r-1)}{2(3q-5)r}}
and
where we used Corollary
4.5
, resulting in expectation bounds of
respective order
\delta_{n}^{-1}
, compensated by an upper bound on the length of the integration domain

\lambda_{n}(1)-\lambda_{n}(0)=\mathcal{O}(\delta_{n})
. Choosing
r
smaller but sufficiently close to

3q/2
, this is bounded by
\frac{K}{\varepsilon}(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-\frac{q(3q-2)}{4(3q-5)}}
, whence

\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{n}=\int_{J_{n}}\bigg|\frac{\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da+o_{\mathbb{P}}((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}).

\bullet
Now we show that
R_{n}
and
\tilde{R}_{n}
are actually negligible, i.e. we prove that
\hat{U}_{n}^{L}
can
be replaced in the previous integral by the following process, where
S_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\delta_{n}^{-1}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})
,

\hat{V}_{n}\colon[\lambda_{n}(0),\lambda_{n}(1)]\to\mathbb{R},\quad\hat{V}_{n}(a)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{|u|\leq S_{n}}\big\{D_{n}(a,u)+W_{\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}^{n}(u)\big\}.

For ease of notation, let us also introduce

\hat{V}_{n}^{*}\colon[\lambda_{n}(0),\lambda_{n}(1)]\to\mathbb{R},\quad\hat{V}_{n}^{*}(a)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{|u|\leq T_{n}}\big\{D_{n}(a,u)+W_{\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}^{n}(u)\big\},

where (
C.7
) guarantees
|u|\leq(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}})^{1/3}L^{n}(t)
for all
|u|\leq T_{n}
.
Note that
\hat{V}_{n}^{*}(a)
differs from
\hat{V}_{n}
if and only if
|\hat{V}_{n}^{*}(a)|>S_{n}
and it
follows from Proposition 1 of
Durot (2002)
together with the comments just before this Proposition, that there
exists
K>0
, such that for every
(x,\alpha)
, satisfying

\alpha\in\big(0,S_{n}\big]
,
x>0
and
K\delta_{n}^{3}S_{n}^{2}\leq-(\alpha\log(2x\alpha))^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|

\displaystyle\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)-\hat{V}_{n}(a)|>\alpha,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)

(C.8)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)-\hat{V}_{n}^{*}(a)|>\alpha/2,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)+\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{V}_{n}(a)-\hat{V}_{n}^{*}(a)|>\alpha/2,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}^{|X}\bigg(2\sup_{|u|\leq T_{n}}|R_{n}(a,u)+\tilde{R}_{n}(a,u)|>x(\alpha/2)^{3/2},\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\bigg)

\displaystyle+KS_{n}x+\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{V}_{n}^{*}(a)|>S_{n},\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)+\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{V}_{n}^{*}(a)|>S_{n},\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)

\displaystyle\leq K(x\alpha^{3/2})^{-q}\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\sup_{|u|\leq T_{n}}|R_{n}(a,u)+\tilde{R}_{n}(a,u)|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]+KS_{n}x+2\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{V}_{n}^{*}(a)|>S_{n},\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big),

where we also applied Markov’s inequality in the last step.
Before deriving an upper bound on the expectation involving
R_{n}
and
\tilde{R}_{n}
, let us consider the
probability involving
\hat{V}_{n}^{*}
. Noting that
D_{n}(a,0)=0
and that a Taylor expansion of

\Lambda_{n}\circ L_{n}^{-1}-aL_{n}^{-1}
around
L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1})
reveals

|D_{n}(a,u)|\geq\delta_{n}^{3/2}\kappa u^{2}
for some
\kappa>0
and
|u|\leq S_{n}
, using that the first
expansion term vanishes, Theorem 4 of
Durot (2002)
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{V}_{n}^{*}(a)|>S_{n},\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)\leq K\exp(-\kappa^{2}\delta_{n}^{3}S_{n}^{3}/2)\leq K\exp(-\kappa^{2}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{3}/2).

(C.9)

By Lemma
F.1
,

\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\sup_{|u|\leq T_{n}}|R_{n}(a,u)+\tilde{R}_{n}(a,u)|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]\leq Kn^{1-q/3}\delta_{n}^{-q/6}

and we obtain together with (
C.8
) and (
C.9
),

\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)-\hat{V}_{n}(a)|>\alpha,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)\leq K(x\alpha^{3/2})^{-q}n^{1-q/3}\delta_{n}^{-q/6}+KS_{n}x

for every
(x,\alpha)
, satisfying
\alpha\in\big(0,S_{n}\big]
,
x>0
and

K\delta_{n}^{3}S_{n}^{2}\leq-(\alpha\log(2x\alpha))^{-1}
. Now note that for any
\varepsilon>0
, for every

\alpha\in((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}\delta_{n}^{-1}/\log(n\delta_{n}^{2}),(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-\varepsilon}\delta_{n}^{-1}]

and

x_{\alpha,n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=S_{n}^{-1/(q+1)}\alpha^{-3q/(2(q+1))}n^{(3-q)/(3(q+1))}\delta_{n}^{-q/(6(q+1))},

we have
(x_{\alpha,n}\alpha^{3/2})^{-q}n^{1-q/3}\delta_{n}^{-q/6}\leq S_{n}x_{\alpha,n}
and

\alpha x_{\alpha,n}\longrightarrow 0
for
n\longrightarrow\infty
and so
(\alpha,x_{\alpha,n})
does in fact satisfy

-(\alpha\log(2x_{\alpha,n}\alpha)^{-1}\geq K\delta_{n}^{3}S_{n}^{2}
. Thus,

\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)-\hat{V}_{n}(a)|>\alpha,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)\leq KS_{n}x_{\alpha,n}
. By definition,
|\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)-\hat{V}_{n}(a)|
is bounded by
2T_{n}
and thus, using that
q>12
,

\displaystyle\int_{J_{n}}\mathbb{E}^{|X}

\displaystyle\big[\big|\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)-\hat{V}_{n}(a)\big|\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\big]da=\int_{J_{n}}\int_{0}^{2T_{n}}\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)-\hat{V}_{n}(a)|>\alpha,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)d\alpha da

\displaystyle\leq K\delta_{n}\bigg((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}\delta_{n}^{-1}/\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})+KT_{n}S_{n}x_{(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-\varepsilon}\delta_{n}^{-1}}+K\int_{(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}\delta_{n}^{-1}/\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}^{(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-\varepsilon}\delta_{n}^{-1}}S_{n}x_{\alpha,n}d\alpha\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq K(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}/\log(n\delta_{n}^{2}).

Consequently, for any
\varepsilon>0
, by Markov’s inequality and Fubini’s theorem,

\mathbb{P}^{|X}\bigg((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\int_{J_{n}}\bigg|\frac{|\hat{U}_{n}^{L}(a)|-|\hat{V}_{n}(a)|}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da>\varepsilon,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\bigg)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

\bullet
In the last step, we approximate the integral over
\hat{V}_{n}
by the integral over

\tilde{V}_{n}\circ\lambda_{n}^{-1}
, where first the integration domain
\mathcal{J}_{n}
can be easily replaced by

[\lambda_{n}(0),\lambda_{n}(1)]
. As the remaining proof is very similar to the one in the previous step, we defer it to
Lemma
F.2
, showing that for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}^{|X}\bigg((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\int_{\lambda_{n}(0)}^{\lambda_{n}(1)}\bigg|\frac{|\hat{V}_{n}(a)|-|\tilde{V}_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))|}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da>\varepsilon,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\bigg)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

A change of variable, where
a
is replaced by
\lambda_{n}(a)
, then proves Claim V.

Claim VI:
The distribution of
(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\big(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{n}-\mu_{n}\big)
under
\mathbb{P}^{|X}
converges weakly in probability to a normal distribution with mean zero and variance
\sigma^{2}
, defined in (
4.1
).

Proof of Claim VI.

As in the proof of Claim V, we show the assertion without loss of generality on
\Omega_{n}^{\prime}
, as

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{n}^{\prime})\longrightarrow 1
for
n\longrightarrow\infty
, with
\Omega_{n}^{\prime}
defined at the beginning of the proof
of Claim V. Let

V_{n}\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{R},\quad V_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\{W_{t}^{n}(u)+d_{n}(t)u^{2}\},

denote a variation of
\tilde{V}_{n}
where the
\operatorname*{argmin}
is now considered over the whole real line instead of

[-S_{n},S_{n}]
, recalling
S_{n}=\delta_{n}^{-1}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})
. Further, define

\eta_{n}\colon[0,1]\to[0,\infty),\quad\eta_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{|\Phi_{n}^{\prime}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(t)|}{(p_{X}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(t))^{2}}

and set

Y_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigg(\bigg|\frac{\tilde{V}_{n}(t)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(t)}\bigg|-\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\bigg|\frac{\tilde{V}_{n}(t)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(t)}\bigg|\bigg]\bigg)\eta_{n}(t)

for
t\in[0,1]
. Note that
V_{n}(t)
can differ from
\tilde{V}_{n}(t)
only if

V_{n}(t)>S_{n}
and so we have by Theorem 4 of
Durot (2002)
that there exists

\kappa>0
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(V_{n}(t)\neq\tilde{V}_{n}(t)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}^{|X}(V_{n}(t)>S_{n})\leq 2\exp(-\kappa^{2}\delta_{n}^{3}S_{n}^{3}/2)=2\exp(-\kappa^{2}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{3}/2).

Note further that under
\mathbb{P}^{|X}
, both
\tilde{V}_{n}(t)
and
V_{n}(t)

have finite second moments and that
\eta_{n}(t)
is bounded. So by Fubini’s theorem and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\bigg|\frac{\tilde{V}_{n}(t)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(t)}\bigg|-\bigg|\frac{V_{n}(t)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(t)}\bigg|\bigg)\eta_{n}(t)dt\bigg]\leq K\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(V_{n}(t)\neq\tilde{V}_{n}(t)\big)^{1/2}dt\leq K(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}/\log(n\delta_{n}^{2}).

Combining this with the fact that
d_{n}(t)^{2/3}V_{n}(t)
is distributed as
X(0)
for any
t
, we have shown that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\int_{0}^{1}\bigg|\frac{\tilde{V}_{n}(t)}{L_{n}^{\prime}(t)}\bigg|\eta_{n}(t)dt\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}[|X(0)|]\int_{0}^{1}\delta_{n}^{-1/3}(L_{n}^{\prime}(t))^{1/3}\bigg(\frac{2}{|\lambda_{n}^{\prime}(t)|}\bigg)^{2/3}\eta_{n}(t)dt+o_{\mathbb{P}}((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{0}^{1}\delta_{n}^{-1/3}\big(4\sigma_{n}^{2}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(t)\big)^{1/3}\bigg(\frac{p_{X}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(t)}{|\Phi_{n}^{\prime}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(t)|}\bigg)^{2/3}\frac{|\Phi_{n}^{\prime}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(t)|}{(p_{X}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(t))^{2}}dt+o_{\mathbb{P}}((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6})

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{-T}^{T}\delta_{n}^{-1/3}\big(4\sigma_{n}^{2}(t)\Phi_{n}^{\prime}(t)\big)^{1/3}p_{X}(t)^{-1/3}dt+o_{\mathbb{P}}((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6})

\displaystyle\quad=\mu_{n}+o_{\mathbb{P}}((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}).

It remains to prove that the distribution of

(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\int_{0}^{1}Y_{n}(t)dt

under
\mathbb{P}^{|X}
converges weakly to
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
in probability, as
n\longrightarrow\infty
. For this, we introduce

v_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Var}^{|X}\bigg((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\int_{0}^{1}Y_{n}(t)dt\bigg)=(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/3}\operatorname{Var}^{|X}\bigg((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\int_{0}^{1}Y_{n}(t)dt\bigg)

and note that similar as in the calculation of
\mu_{n}
in the previous display and by virtually the same arguments as in Step 5 of
Durot (2008)
, we obtain

v_{n}=\sigma^{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Asymptotic normality of
(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\int_{0}^{1}Y_{n}(t)dt
can now be deduced as in Step 6 of

Durot (2007)
by Bernstein’s method of big blocks and small blocks, where the only difference lies in the
replacement of
n
by
n\delta_{n}^{2}
.

Conclusion:
By combining Claims I – VI,

\displaystyle(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{n}-\mu_{n}\Big)

\displaystyle=(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}\big(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{n}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})\big)-\mu_{n}\Big)

\displaystyle=(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{1/3}\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{n}-\mu_{n}\Big)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\longrightarrow_{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})

for
n\longrightarrow\infty
, unconditionally under
\mathbb{P}
.
∎

D
Proof of the auxiliary result of Section
7

Lemma D.1
.

Let
A_{n}\colon[-T,T]\to\mathbb{R}
denote the continuous, piecewise linear process satisfying

A_{n}(X_{i})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}(Y_{\ell}^{n}-\Phi_{0}(0))\big(1-2\mathds{1}_{\{X_{\ell}\leq X_{i}\}}\big)

for
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
. Then, as
n\delta_{n}^{2}\longrightarrow 0
and
n\longrightarrow\infty
,

A_{n}\longrightarrow_{\mathcal{L}}A\ \text{ in }\mathcal{C}([-T,T]),

where
A
is defined in Theorem
4.3
(ii) and where
\mathcal{C}([-T,T])

denotes the space of continuous functions
\mathcal{C}([-T,T])
endowed with the topology of uniform convergence.

As the processes
A_{n}
are already continuous, we may rely on the classical theory of weak convergence on
Polish spaces. To this aim, we need to prove convergence of finite-dimensional distributions to
A
, as well as tightness of the sequence

(A_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\mathcal{C}([-T,T])
(cf. Theorem 7.3 in
Billingsley (1999)
). But first,
for any
s\in[-T,T]
, let
i(s)\in\{0,\dots,n\}
be the random index that satisfies

X_{i(s)}\leq s<X_{i(s)+1}
. Then,

\displaystyle A_{n}(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\Big\{(Y_{\ell}^{n}-\Phi_{0}(0))\big(1-2\mathds{1}_{\{X_{\ell}\leq X_{i(s)}\}}\big)+(s-X_{i(s)})\big(A_{n}(X_{i(s)+1})-A_{n}(X_{i(s)})\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}(Y_{\ell}^{n}-\Phi_{0}(0))\big(1-2\mathds{1}_{\{X_{\ell}\leq s\}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\frac{(s-X_{i(s)})}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}(Y_{\ell}^{n}-\Phi_{0}(0))\big(\mathds{1}_{\{X_{\ell}\leq X_{i(s)}\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X_{\ell}\leq X_{i(s)+1}\}}\big).

Note that

\sup_{s\in[-T,T]}\bigg|\frac{(s-X_{i(s)})}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}(Y_{\ell}^{n}-\Phi_{0}(0))\big(\mathds{1}_{\{X_{\ell}\leq X_{i(s)}\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X_{\ell}\leq X_{i(s)+1}\}}\big)\bigg|\leq\frac{2T}{\sqrt{n}}=o(1).

Hence, it suffices to show convergence of the finite-dimensional distributions and uniform stochastic equicontinuity of the
processes

\mathfrak{A}_{n}(\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}(Y_{\ell}^{n}-\Phi_{0}(0))\big(1-2\mathds{1}_{\{X_{\ell}\leq\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\}}\big),

implying convergence of the finite dimensional distributions and tightness of
A_{n}
.

Convergence of finite-dimensional distributions.

For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{k}\}\subset[-T,T]
denote a subset of
cardinality
k
, define

f_{n}\colon[-T,T]\times\{0,1\}\times[-T,T]\to\mathbb{R},\quad f_{n}(x,y,s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(y-\Phi_{0}(0))(1-2\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq s\}})

and note that

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{A}_{n}(s_{1})\\
\vdots\\
\mathfrak{A}_{n}(s_{k})\end{pmatrix}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\begin{pmatrix}f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},s_{1})\\
\vdots\\
f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},s_{k})\end{pmatrix},

as well as that
\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},s)]=o(n^{-1/2})
.
As a shorthand notation, let us introduce

\displaystyle V_{i}^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\begin{pmatrix}f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},s_{1})\\
\vdots\\
f_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n},s_{k})\end{pmatrix}

for
i=1,\dots,n
. Note that
\|V_{i}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}\leq k/n
by definition of
f_{n}

and
b_{n}
and so for every
\varepsilon>0
,

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\|V_{i}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}_{\{\|V_{i}^{n}\|_{2}>\varepsilon\}}]\leq\frac{k}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\{\|V_{i}^{n}\|_{2}^{2}>\varepsilon^{2}\}}]\leq\frac{k}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\{k>n\varepsilon^{2}\}}]=k\mathds{1}_{\{k>n\varepsilon^{2}\}}\longrightarrow 0

as
n\longrightarrow\infty
. Next, for

j,\ell\in\{1,\dots,k\}
, we evaluate

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{Cov}(

\displaystyle V_{i}^{n})\bigg)_{j\ell}=\mathbb{E}\big[(Y^{n}-\Phi_{0}(0))^{2}(1-2\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq s_{j}\}}\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq s_{\ell}\}}\big]+o(1)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{0}(0)(1-\Phi_{0}(0))\mathbb{E}[1-2\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq s_{j}\}}-2\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq s_{\ell}\}}+4\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}\}}]+o(1)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{0}(0)(1-\Phi_{0}(0))(1+4F_{X}(\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\})-2F_{X}(s_{j})-2F_{X}(s_{\ell}))+o(1)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{0}(0)(1-\Phi_{0}(0))(1+2F_{X}(\min\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\})-2F_{X}(\max\{s_{j},s_{\ell}\}))+o(1)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{0}(0)(1-\Phi_{0}(0))(1-2|F_{X}(s_{\ell})-F_{X}(s_{j})|)+o(1)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Cov}(A(s_{j}),A(s_{\ell}))+o(1).

The Lindeberg-Feller central limit theorem then implies

(\mathfrak{A}_{n}(s_{1}),\dots,\mathfrak{A}_{n}(s_{k}))\longrightarrow_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathfrak{A}(s_{1}),\dots,\mathfrak{A}(s_{k}))\quad\text{as }n\longrightarrow\infty.

Uniform stochastic equicontinuity of
(\mathfrak{A}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Note that convergence of finite dimensional distributions implies tightness of
(\mathfrak{A}_{n}(s))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
for every

s\in[-T,T]
.
To show that
(\mathfrak{A}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is asymptotically uniformly equicontinuous in probability, let
\varepsilon>0
and
\eta>0

and note that by Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{|s-t|<\eta}|\mathfrak{A}_{n}(s)-\mathfrak{A}_{n}(t)|>\Delta\bigg)\leq\frac{1}{\Delta}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|s-t|<\eta}|\mathfrak{A}_{n}(s)-\mathfrak{A}_{n}(t)|\bigg].

(D.1)

Defining

h_{n,s,t}\colon[-T,T]\times\{0,1\}\to\mathbb{R},\qquad h_{n,s,t}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=2(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))(\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq t\}})

for
s,t\in[-T,T]
, setting
\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{h_{n,s,t}\mid s,t\in[-T,T],|s-t|<\eta\}
and choosing

\varepsilon_{n}>0
and
M_{n}>0
, such that
\mathbb{E}[h^{2}]<\varepsilon_{n}^{2}
and
\|h\|_{\infty}\leq M_{n}
for every

h\in\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta}
, we have by Theorem 2.14.17’ of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|s-t|<\eta}|\mathfrak{A}_{n}(s)-\mathfrak{A}_{n}(t)|\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{h_{n}\in\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n})-\mathbb{E}[h_{n}(X_{i},Y_{i}^{n})]\bigg|\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq J_{[]}\big(\varepsilon_{n},\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\bigg(1+\frac{J_{[]}\big(\varepsilon_{n},\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)}{\varepsilon_{n}^{2}n^{1/2}}M_{n}\bigg).

For arbitrary
h\in\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta}
, there exists
s,t\in[-T,T]
, satisfying
|s-t|<\eta
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[h(X,Y^{n})^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[h_{n,s,t}(X,Y^{n})^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[(\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{X\leq t\}})^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\{\min\{s,t\}<X\leq\max\{s,t\}\}}]

\displaystyle=F_{X}(\max\{s,t\})-F_{X}(\min\{s,t\})

\displaystyle\leq\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}(\max\{s,t\}-\min\{s,t\})

\displaystyle=\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}|s-t|<\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}\eta

and
\|h\|_{\infty}=\|h_{n,s,t}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Thus, by choosing
\varepsilon_{n}=\sqrt{\eta}
and
M_{n}=1
, we
have

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|s-t|<\eta}|\mathfrak{A}_{n}(s)-\mathfrak{A}_{n}(t)|\bigg]\leq J_{[]}\big(\eta^{1/2},\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\bigg(1+\frac{J_{[]}\big(\sqrt{\eta},\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)}{\eta\sqrt{n}}\bigg).

By similar arguments as in Lemma
G.7
(iv), it follows that for some constant
K>0
, which may change from
line to line,

N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}}))\leq\frac{K}{\nu^{4}}.

Thus, by utilizing that
\frac{d}{dx}x(\log(K/x^{4})+4)=\log(K/x^{4})
,

J_{[]}\big(\sqrt{\eta},\mathcal{H}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\leq K\int_{0}^{\sqrt{\eta}}\log\Big(\frac{K}{\nu^{4}}\Big)d\nu\leq K\sqrt{\eta}\log\Big(\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\Big).

Therefore, the assertion follows from (
D.1
) combined with

\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|s-t|<\eta}|\mathfrak{A}_{n}(s)-\mathfrak{A}_{n}(t)|\bigg]\leq K\sqrt{\eta}\log\Big(\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\Big).

∎

E
Proofs of Section
4.3

This section contains the proofs of Lemma
4.4
and Corollary
4.5
.

E.1
Proof of Lemma
4.4

The result follows immediately for the case
n\delta_{n}^{2}\longrightarrow 0
, as in this case, for
n
large enough,
the right-hand side is greater than
1
. For
n\delta_{n}^{2}\longrightarrow c\in(0,\infty]
, the proof follows
the route of Theorem 1 in
Durot (2007)
but incorporates the explicit dependence on
\Phi_{n}^{\prime}
and thus
reveals the convergence rate of the inverse process in the weak-feature-impact scenario.
The first and last inequalities in both cases are obviously true. So for the proof of (i), let us consider

x\in[(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3},1]
and let
K>0
denote a constant which may changes from line to line. Let us define

M_{n}\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
,
M_{n}(t)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Upsilon_{n}(t)-\Lambda_{n}(t)
, set

\varepsilon_{(j)}^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=Y_{j}^{n}-\Phi_{n}(X_{(j)})
and note that by definition of
\Upsilon_{n}
, we have

\Upsilon_{n}(u)=\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor nu\rfloor}{n}\bigg)+\bigg(u-\frac{\lfloor nu\rfloor}{n}\bigg)\bigg(\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor nu\rfloor+1}{n}\bigg)-\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor nu\rfloor}{n}\bigg)\bigg),

where

\Upsilon_{n}(i/n)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{i}Y_{(j)}^{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\varepsilon_{(j)}^{n}+\int_{0}^{i/n}\Phi_{n}(F_{n}^{-1}(u))du,\quad i=1,\dots,n.

Now fix
a\in\mathbb{R}
and note that by definition of
\tilde{U}_{n}
,

\big\{|\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|\geq x\big\}\subset\bigg\{\inf_{|u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|\geq x}\Upsilon_{n}(u)-au\leq\Upsilon_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-a\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\bigg\}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|>x\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\inf_{|u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|\geq x}\Upsilon_{n}(u)-au\leq\Upsilon_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-a\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\inf_{|u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|\geq x}\Upsilon_{n}(u)-\Upsilon_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))+a\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-au\leq 0\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{|u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|\geq x}\Upsilon_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-\Upsilon_{n}(u)+au-a\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\geq 0\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{|u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|\geq x}M_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-M_{n}(u)+\Lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-\Lambda_{n}(u)+au-a\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\geq 0\bigg).

From a Taylor expansion of
\Lambda_{n}(u)
around
\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)
with Lagrange remainder, we obtain

\Lambda_{n}(u)=\Lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))+\lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))(u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))+\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{n}^{\prime}(\xi_{n})(u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))^{2}

for some
\xi_{n}
between
u
and
\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)
and by assumption, we know that at least for
n
large enough,

\lambda_{n}^{\prime}(t)=\delta_{n}\Phi_{0}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}F_{X}^{-1}(t))(F_{X}^{-1})^{\prime}(t)>\delta_{n}K.

Thus,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-\Lambda_{n}(u)

\displaystyle=-\lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))(u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{n}^{\prime}(\xi_{n})(u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))(u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-K\delta_{n}(u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))^{2}.

Now note that if
\lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\neq a
, then either

a<\lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\quad\text{and}\quad\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)=F_{X}(-T)=0,

or

a>\lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\quad\text{and}\quad\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)=F_{X}(T)=1.

Thus,
(a-\lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)))(u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\leq 0
for every
a
and we obtain

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-\Lambda_{n}(u)+au-a\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq-\lambda_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))(u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-K\delta_{n}(u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))^{2}+a(u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq-K\delta_{n}(u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))^{2}.

Consequently, by a slicing argument, the union bound and Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|\geq x\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{|u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|\geq x}M_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-M_{n}(u)-K\delta_{n}(u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))^{2}\geq 0\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\geq 0}\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{|u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|\in[x2^{k},x2^{k+1}]}M_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-M_{n}(u)\geq K\delta_{n}(x2^{k})^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq K(\delta_{n}x^{2})^{-q}\sum_{k\geq 0}2^{-2kq}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|\in[x2^{k},x2^{k+1}]}|M_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-M_{n}(u)|^{q}\bigg].

Now we want to determine an upper bound for the expectation in the previous inequality. For
u\in[0,1]
and without loss
of generality for
t\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{n}(t+u)-\Upsilon_{n}(u)

\displaystyle=\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor}{n}\bigg)-\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor nu\rfloor}{n}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\bigg(t+u-\frac{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor}{n}\bigg)\bigg(\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor+1}{n}\bigg)-\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor}{n}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\bigg(u-\frac{\lfloor nu\rfloor}{n}\bigg)\bigg(\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor nu\rfloor+1}{n}\bigg)-\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor nu\rfloor}{n}\bigg)\bigg).

As an immediate consequence, we see

\bigg(t+u-\frac{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor}{n}\bigg)\leq\frac{1}{n}\quad\text{and}\quad\bigg(u-\frac{\lfloor nu\rfloor}{n}\bigg)\leq\frac{1}{n}.

By definition of
\Upsilon_{n}
, we find

\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor}{n}\bigg)-\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor nu\rfloor}{n}\bigg)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=\lfloor nu\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor}\varepsilon_{(j)}^{n}+\int_{\lfloor nu\rfloor/n}^{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor/n}\Phi_{n}(F_{n}^{-1}(s))ds

and in particular,

\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor+1}{n}\bigg)-\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor}{n}\bigg)=\frac{1}{n}Y_{(\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor+1)}^{n}

and

\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor nu\rfloor+1}{n}\bigg)-\Upsilon_{n}\bigg(\frac{\lfloor nu\rfloor}{n}\bigg)=\frac{1}{n}Y_{(\lfloor nu\rfloor+1)}^{n}.

Putting all of this together, we have by definition of
\Lambda_{n}
and the mean value theorem,

\displaystyle|M_{n}(t+u)-M_{n}(u)|

\displaystyle=|\Upsilon_{n}(t+u)-\Upsilon_{n}(u)-(\Lambda_{n}(t+u)-\Lambda_{n}(u))|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=\lfloor nu\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor}\varepsilon_{(j)}^{n}+\int_{\lfloor nu\rfloor/n}^{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor/n}\Phi_{n}(F_{n}^{-1}(s))ds-\int_{u}^{t+u}\lambda_{n}(s)ds\Bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{n^{2}}(Y_{(\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor+1)}^{n}+Y_{(\lfloor nu\rfloor+1)}^{n})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\Bigg|\sum_{j=\lfloor nu\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor}\varepsilon_{(j)}^{n}\Bigg|+\bigg|\int_{u}^{t+u}\Phi_{n}(F_{n}^{-1}(s))-\Phi_{n}(F_{X}^{-1}(s))ds\bigg|+\frac{2}{n}+\frac{2}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\Bigg|\sum_{j=\lfloor nu\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor}\varepsilon_{(j)}^{n}\Bigg|+\delta_{n}t\sup_{s\in[-T,T]}|\Phi_{0}^{\prime}(\delta_{n}s)|\sup_{v\in[0,1]}|F_{n}^{-1}(v)-F_{X}^{-1}(v)|+\frac{4}{n}.

For
x\geq 1/n
, which follows from
x\geq(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3}
, we observe from the previous inequality,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,x]}|M_{n}(t+u)-M_{n}(u)|\leq\frac{1}{n}\sup_{t\in[0,x]}\Bigg|\sum_{j=\lfloor nu\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor}\varepsilon_{(j)}^{n}\Bigg|+K\delta_{n}x\sup_{v\in[0,1]}|F_{n}^{-1}(v)-F_{X}^{-1}(v)|+\frac{4}{n}.

From
Dvoretzky et al. (1956)
,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{v\in[0,1]}|F_{n}^{-1}(v)-F_{X}^{-1}(v)|^{q}\bigg]\leq Kn^{-q/2}.

The following bound

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,x]}\Bigg|\sum_{j=\lfloor nu\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor n(t+u)\rfloor}\varepsilon_{(j)}^{n}\Bigg|^{q}\Bigg]\leq K(nx)^{q/2}

is virtually the same as on p. 333 in
Durot (2008)
and can be likewise deduced by Doob’s inequality together
with Theorem 3 of
Rosenthal (1973)
, noting that the arguments do not involve the level of feature impact

\delta_{n}
. Finally, this shows

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,x]}\big|M_{n}(t+u)-M_{n}(u)\big|^{q}\bigg]^{1/q}\leq K\frac{x^{1/2}}{\sqrt{n}}+K\frac{x\delta_{n}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{4}{n}

and for
n
sufficiently large, we have
1/n\leq\delta_{n}/\sqrt{n}\leq 1/\sqrt{n}
and by utilizing

x\geq 1/n
,

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,x]}\big|M_{n}(t+u)-M_{n}(u)\big|^{q}\bigg]\leq K\Big(\frac{x}{n}\Big)^{q/2}.

By using the same arguments, this also holds for
t\in[-1,0]
and so we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|u-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|\in[x2^{k},x2^{k+1}]}\big|M_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))-M_{n}(u)\big|^{q}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{|\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-u|\leq x2^{k+1}}\big|M_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-u+u)-M_{n}(u)\big|^{q}\bigg]\leq K\bigg(\frac{x2^{k+1}}{n}\bigg)^{q/2}.

Combining this with the previous results, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)|\geq x\big)

\displaystyle\leq K(\delta_{n}x^{2})^{-q}\sum_{k\geq 0}2^{-2kq}\bigg(\frac{x2^{k+1}}{n}\bigg)^{q/2}\leq K(n\delta_{n}^{2}x^{3})^{-q/2}\sum_{k\geq 0}2^{-3kq/2}

\displaystyle\leq K(n\delta_{n}^{2}x^{3})^{-q/2}

and statement (i) follows.

For the proof of statement (ii), let
x\in[(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3},2T]
and let again
K>0
denote a constant that may
changes from line to line. Note further that a Taylor expansion with Lagrange remainder of
F_{X}^{-1}
around

\tilde{U}_{n}(a)
yields

F_{X}^{-1}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))=F_{X}^{-1}(\tilde{U}_{n}(a))+(F_{X}^{-1})^{\prime}(\xi_{n})(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-\tilde{U}_{n}(a))

for some
\xi_{n}
between
\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)
and
\tilde{U}_{n}(a)
. Consequently,

\displaystyle|F_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{U}_{n}(a))-F_{X}^{-1}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))|

\displaystyle\leq|F_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{U}_{n}(a))-F_{X}^{-1}(\tilde{U}_{n}(a))|+|(F_{X}^{-1})^{\prime}(\xi_{n})(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-\tilde{U}_{n}(a))|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{u\in[0,1]}|F_{n}^{-1}(u)-F_{X}^{-1}(u)|+\frac{1}{p_{X}(F_{X}^{-1}(\xi_{n}))}|\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-\tilde{U}_{n}(a))|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{u\in[0,1]}|F_{n}^{-1}(u)-F_{X}^{-1}(u)|+\Big(\inf_{t\in[-T,T]}p_{X}(t)\Big)^{-1}|\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)-\tilde{U}_{n}(a)|

and we obtain from statement (i), Markov’s inequality and
Dvoretzky et al. (1956)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|F_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{U}_{n}(a))-\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)|\geq x\big)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\big(|F_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{U}_{n}(a))-F_{X}^{-1}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))|\geq x\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\big|F_{n}^{-1}(u)-F_{X}^{-1}(u)\big|\geq x/2\bigg)+\mathbb{P}\big(\big|F_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))-\tilde{U}_{n}(a)\big|\geq Kx\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq Kx^{-3q/2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\big|F_{n}^{-1}(u)-F_{X}^{-1}(u)\big|^{3q/2}\bigg]+K(n\delta_{n}^{2}x^{3})^{-q/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq Kx^{-3q/2}\big(n^{-3q/4}+(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-q/2}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq K(n\delta_{n}^{2}x^{3})^{-q/2},
