which proves statement (ii). ∎

The next result is a variation of Lemma
4.4
for the case that

a\in[0,1]\setminus\Phi_{n}([-T,T])
. The proof follows exactly the lines of Lemma 2 in
Durot (2008)
and
is therefore omitted.

Lemma E.1
.

There exist constants
C=C(\Phi_{0},F_{X})>0
and
N_{0}=N_{0}(\Phi_{0})\in\mathbb{N}
, such
that for every
n\geq N_{0}
,
a\in[0,1]\setminus\Phi_{n}([-T,T])
and
x>0
,

(i)

\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\big|\geq x\big)\leq K(nx)^{-1}(\Phi_{n}\circ\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)-a)^{-2}
,

(ii)

\mathbb{P}\big(\big|F_{n}^{-1}\circ\tilde{U}_{n}(a)-\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)\big|\geq x\big)\leq K(nx)^{-1}(\Phi_{n}\circ\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a)-a)^{-2}
.

E.2
Proof of Corollary
4.5

Note first that by monotonicity of
\tilde{U}_{n}
and
\lambda_{n}^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a+Z_{1,n})\hskip-0.15pt-\hskip-0.15pt\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a+Z_{2,n})\big|

\displaystyle=\max\big\{\tilde{U}_{n}(a+Z_{1,n})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a+Z_{2,n}),\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a+Z_{2,n})-\tilde{U}_{n}(a+Z_{1,n})\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\big\{\tilde{U}_{n}(a+c_{n})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a-c_{n}),\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a+c_{n})-\tilde{U}_{n}(a-c_{n})\big\}.

Thus, for any
x>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a+Z_{1,n})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a+Z_{2,n})\big|>x\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a+c_{n})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a-c_{n})\big|>x/2\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a-c_{n})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a+c_{n})\big|>x/2\big).

Note further that by a Taylor expansion of
\lambda_{n}^{-1}
around
a-c_{n}
,

|\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a+c_{n})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a-c_{n})|\leq Kc_{n}\delta_{n}^{-1}

for some
K>0
, depending only on the bounds on
\Phi_{0}^{\prime}(0)
and
p_{X}
. Thus, by a suitable redefinition of
K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a+c_{n})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a-c_{n})\big|>x/2\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a+c_{n})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a-c_{n})\big|>x/4\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\big|\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a+c_{n})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a-c_{n})\big|>x/4\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[0,4(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3})\}}+K(n\delta_{n}^{2}x^{3})^{-q/2}\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[4(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3},1]\}}+\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[0,Kc_{n}\delta_{n}^{-1}]\}},

by Lemma
4.4
(i) and similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a-c_{n})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a+c_{n})\big|>x/2\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[0,4(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3})\}}+K(n\delta_{n}^{2}x^{3})^{-q/2}\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[4(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/3},1]\}}+\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[0,Kc_{n}\delta_{n}^{-1}]\}}.

Integrating
\mathbb{P}(|\tilde{U}_{n}(a+Z_{1,n})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a+Z_{2,n})|>x)
over
x
now yields, again for a redefined

K
,

\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\tilde{U}_{n}(a+Z_{1,n})-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a+Z_{2,n})\big|^{r}\big]\leq K\min\Big\{(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-r/3}+\Big(\frac{c_{n}}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{r},1\Big\}.

∎

F
Proofs of the auxiliary results of Section
C.3

Lemma F.1
.

Under the same assumptions as in Theorem
4.3
(i) and by using the same notations as in Section
C.3
, we have

\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\sup_{|u|\leq T_{n}}|R_{n}(a,u)+\tilde{R}_{n}(a,u)|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]\leq Kn^{1-q/3}\delta_{n}^{-q/6}.

By definition of
\tilde{R}_{n}
, by definition of
\Omega_{n}^{\prime}
, by the Minkowski’s inequality and by the classical bound
on the expected modulus of continuity of Brownian motion (e.g. formula (2) in
Fischer and Nappo (2010)
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\sup_{|u|\leq T_{n}}|\tilde{R}_{n}(a,u)|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{2/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\bigg|\Upsilon_{n}(t)-\int_{0}^{t}\Phi_{n}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(x)dx-\frac{W_{n}(L^{n}(t))}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg|^{q}\bigg]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\sup_{|u|\leq T_{n}}\bigg|\frac{n^{1/6}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}W_{n}\Big(L^{n}\Big(L_{n}^{-1}\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}u+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\Big)\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{n^{1/6}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}W_{n}\big(L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\big)-\big(1-\psi_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\big)^{1/2}W_{\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}^{n}(u)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\big(1-(1-\psi_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)))^{1/2}\big)W_{\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}^{n}(u)\bigg|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq A\frac{n^{2/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}n^{(1-q)/q}+\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\sup_{|u|\leq T_{n}}\bigg|\frac{n^{1/6}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}W_{n}\Big(L^{n}\Big(L_{n}^{-1}\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}u+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\Big)\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{n^{1/6}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}W_{n}\Big(L^{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))+\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}u\big(1-\psi_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\big)\Big)\bigg|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\sup_{|u|\leq T_{n}}\bigg|\psi_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))W_{\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}^{n}(u)\bigg|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leq A\frac{n^{1/q-1/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}+\frac{n^{1/6}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\sup_{|u-v|\leq(n/\delta_{n})^{-1/3}T_{n}(\log(n)/\sqrt{n})\delta_{n}}\big|W_{n}(v)-W_{n}(u)\big|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\big|W_{\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}^{n}(u)\big|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]^{1/q}\frac{K\log(n)}{n^{1/2}}\delta_{n}

\displaystyle\leq K\frac{n^{1/q-1/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}+T_{n}^{1/2}\frac{\log(n)}{n^{1/2}}\delta_{n}+\frac{K\log(n)}{n^{1/2}}\delta_{n}

\displaystyle\leq Kn^{1/q-1/3}\delta_{n}^{-1/6}

and by definition of
a_{n}^{B}
in (
C.2
) and Minkowski’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\sup_{|u|\leq T_{n}}|R_{n}(a,u)|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{n^{2/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[\sup_{|u|\leq T_{n}}\bigg|\int_{\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)}^{L_{n}^{-1}((\frac{n}{\delta_{n}})^{-1/3}u+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)))}\Phi_{n}\circ F_{X}^{-1}(x)-\Phi_{n}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\Phi_{n}^{\prime}(x)\frac{B_{n}(F_{X}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(x))}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(x)}+\Phi_{n}^{\prime}(x)\frac{B_{n}(F_{X}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(x))}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(x)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{B_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a_{n}))}{\sqrt{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1})^{\prime}(a_{n})}dx\bigg|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{n^{1/3}}{\delta_{n}^{1/6}}\sup_{|u|\leq T_{n}}\Big|L_{n}^{-1}\Big(\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/3}u+L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))\Big)-\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a)\Big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\bigg(\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[K\delta_{n}^{q}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\bigg|F_{X}^{-1}(t)-F_{n}^{-1}(t)-\frac{B_{n}(F_{X}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(t))}{\sqrt{n}p_{X}\circ F_{n}^{-1}(t)}\bigg|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\mathbb{E}^{|X}\bigg[K\delta_{n}^{q}n^{-q/2}\sup_{|v-w|\leq(n/\delta_{n})^{-1/3}T_{n}}\big|B_{n}(w)-B_{n}(v)\big|^{q}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\bigg]^{1/q}\bigg).

By a Taylor expansion of
L_{n}^{-1}
around
L_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))
and the definition of
\Omega_{n}^{\prime}
, the
right-hand side of the previous display is bounded by

Kn^{1/3}\delta_{n}^{1/6}\delta_{n}T_{n}\Big(n^{-1}\log(n)^{2}+n^{-1/2}\Big(\frac{n}{\delta_{n}}\Big)^{-1/6}T_{n}^{1/2}\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/2}\Big)\leq Kn^{1/q-1/3}\delta_{n}^{-1/6}.

∎

Lemma F.2
.

Under the same assumptions as in Theorem
4.3
(i) and by using the same notations as in Section
C.3
, we have for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}^{|X}\bigg((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\int_{\lambda_{n}(0)}^{\lambda_{n}(1)}\bigg|\frac{|\hat{V}_{n}(a)|-|\tilde{V}_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))|}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da>\varepsilon,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\bigg)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

By a Taylor expansion, there exists
K>0
, such that for all
|u|\leq S_{n}
,

|D_{n}(a,u)-d_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))u^{2}|\leq Kn^{-1/3}\delta_{n}^{-1/6}\delta_{n}^{2}S_{n}^{3}.

By similar arguments as in the third step of the proof of Claim V in the proof of Theorem
4.3
(i), we have by Proposition 1 of
Durot (2002)
and Theorem 4 of
Durot (2002)
,
for every
(x,\alpha)
, satisfying
\alpha\in\big(0,S_{n}\big]
,
x>0
and

K\delta_{n}^{3}S_{n}^{2}\leq-(\alpha\log(2x\alpha))^{-1}
, that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{V}_{n}(a)

\displaystyle-\tilde{V}_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))|>\alpha,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}^{|X}\bigg(2\sup_{|u|\leq S_{n}}|D_{n}(a,u)-d_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))u^{2}|>x\alpha^{3/2},\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+KS_{n}x+\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{V}_{n}(a)|>S_{n},\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathds{1}_{\{Kn^{-1/3}\delta_{n}^{-1/6}\delta_{n}^{2}S_{n}^{3}>x\alpha^{3/2}\}}+KS_{n}x+K\exp(-\kappa^{2}\delta_{n}^{3}S_{n}^{3}/2).

For any
\varepsilon>0
, every

\alpha\in((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}\delta_{n}^{-1}/\log(n\delta_{n}^{2}),(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-\varepsilon}\delta_{n}^{-1}]

and

x_{\alpha,n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=2K\alpha^{-3/2}n^{-1/3}\delta_{n}^{-1/6}\delta_{n}^{2}S_{n}^{3},

we have
\alpha x_{\alpha,n}\longrightarrow 0
for
n\longrightarrow\infty
and so
(\alpha,x_{\alpha,n})
satisfies

-(\alpha\log(2x_{\alpha,n}\alpha))^{-1}\geq K\delta_{n}^{3}S_{n}^{2}

for
n
large enough. Thus, again for
n
large enough,

\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{V}_{n}(a)-\tilde{V}_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))|>\alpha,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)\leq KS_{n}x_{\alpha,n}

for every

\alpha\in((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}\delta_{n}^{-1}/\log(n\delta_{n}^{2}),(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-\varepsilon}\delta_{n}^{-1}]
.
By definition,
|\hat{V}_{n}(a)-\tilde{V}_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))|
is bounded by
2S_{n}
and so we obtain,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{|X}\big[|\hat{V}_{n}(a)-\tilde{V}_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))|\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\big]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{2S_{n}}\mathbb{P}^{|X}\big(|\hat{V}_{n}(a)-\tilde{V}_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))|>\alpha,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\big)d\alpha

\displaystyle\leq K(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}\delta_{n}^{-1}/\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})+KS_{n}x_{(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-\varepsilon}\delta_{n}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+K\int_{(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}\delta_{n}^{-1}/\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})}^{(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-\varepsilon}\delta_{n}^{-1}}S_{n}x_{\alpha,n}d\alpha

\displaystyle\leq K(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}\delta_{n}^{-1}/\log(n\delta_{n}^{2}).

Thus,

\displaystyle(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\int_{\lambda_{n}(0)}^{\lambda_{n}(1)}\mathbb{E}^{|X}\big[|\hat{V}_{n}(a)

\displaystyle-\tilde{V}_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))|\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{n}^{\prime}}\big]da\leq K(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\delta_{n}(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1/6}\delta_{n}^{-1}/\log(n\delta_{n}^{2}),

which is bounded by
K\log(n\delta_{n}^{2})^{-1}
and, as desired for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\mathbb{P}^{|X}\bigg((n\delta_{n}^{2})^{1/6}\int_{\lambda_{n}(0)}^{\lambda_{n}(1)}\bigg|\frac{|\hat{V}_{n}(a)|-|\tilde{V}_{n}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))|}{L_{n}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n}^{-1}(a))}\bigg|\frac{1}{p_{X}(\Phi_{n}^{-1}(a))}da>\varepsilon,\Omega_{n}^{\prime}\bigg)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

∎

G
Auxiliary results

In this section, we summarize some necessary technical auxiliary results. Throughout, we use the notations introduced in Sections
1
–
F
.

Lemma G.1
.

For
a>0
and
b\geq 0
, we have

\frac{\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b}}{\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{2}}-\sqrt{b}}=2\frac{\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{2}}+\sqrt{b}}{\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}},\quad\frac{\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{2}}+\sqrt{b}}{\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}}\leq 2,\quad\big|\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b}\big|^{2}\leq 16\Bigg|\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{2}}-\sqrt{b}\Bigg|^{2}.

The first statement follows from
(\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b})(\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b})=a-b
and

2\Bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{2}}+\sqrt{b}\Bigg)\Bigg(\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{2}}-\sqrt{b}\Bigg)=2\Big(\frac{a+b}{2}-b\Big)=a+b-2b=a-b.

For the second statement, note that

\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{2}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{a}{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{b}{2}}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}.

Thus,

\frac{\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{2}}+\sqrt{b}}{\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{a}+2\sqrt{b}}{\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}}\leq 2\frac{\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}}{\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}}=2.

By a combination of the first two statements, we obtain

\big|\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b}\big|=2\Bigg|\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{2}}-\sqrt{b}\Bigg|\Bigg(\frac{\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{2}}+\sqrt{b}}{\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}}\Bigg)\leq 4\Bigg|\sqrt{\frac{a+b}{2}}-\sqrt{b}\Bigg|

and the third statement follows from taking squares on both sides.
∎

Lemma G.2
.

For
a,b\in[0,\infty)
, we have

(i)

|\log(1/2+b)-\log(1/2+a)|\leq 2|b-a|
,

(ii)

For
a\in(0,\infty)
, we have
\log(a)\leq 2(\sqrt{a}-1)
and for
a\geq 1
, we have
\log(a)^{2}\leq 4(\sqrt{a}-1)^{2}
,

(iii)

For
0<a\leq 1
, we have
\log(a)^{2}\leq(1-\frac{1}{a})^{2}
.

(i)

Without loss of generality, we assume
a\leq b
. Then,

\displaystyle|\log(1/2+b)-\log(1/2+a)|=\log\Big(\frac{1/2+b}{1/2+a}\Big)=\log\Big(1+\Big(\frac{1/2+b}{1/2+a}-1\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{1/2+b}{1/2+a}-1=\frac{1}{1/2+a}(1/2+b-(1/2+a))=\frac{1}{1+2a}2(b-a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq 2|b-a|,

where we used
\log(1+x)\leq x
for
x\in[0,\infty)
.

(ii)

Let
g\colon(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
,
g(a)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\log(a)-2(\sqrt{a}-1)
. Then,
g(1)=0
and

g^{\prime}(a)=\frac{1}{a}-2\frac{1}{2\sqrt{a}}=\frac{1-\sqrt{a}}{a}.

Thus,
g^{\prime}(a)>0
for
a\in(0,1)
,
g^{\prime}(1)=0
and
g^{\prime}(a)<0
for
a>1
, implying

\log(a)\leq 2(\sqrt{a}-1).

The assertion now follows from the fact that
\log(a)\geq 0
and
2(\sqrt{a}-1)\geq 0
for all
a\geq 1
.

(iii)

Let
g\colon(0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
,
g(a)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\log(a)-1+\frac{1}{a}
. Then,
g(1)=0
and

g^{\prime}(a)=\frac{1}{a}-\frac{1}{a^{2}}=\frac{a-1}{a^{2}}\leq 0.

Thus,
g(a)\geq 0
for all
a\in(0,1]
, implying

\log(a)\geq 1-\frac{1}{a}.

The assertion now follows from the fact that
\log(a)\leq 0
and
2(\sqrt{a}-1)\leq 0
for all
a\leq 1
.

∎

Lemma G.3
.

Let
G\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
and assume there exists
T\in\mathbb{R}
with
G|_{(-\infty,T)}<0
and
G|_{[T,\infty)}\geq 0
.
Then, for every
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\int_{s}^{\infty}G(x)dx-\int_{-\infty}^{s}G(x)dx\leq\int_{T}^{\infty}G(x)dx-\int_{-\infty}^{T}G(x)dx.

In particular,

\max_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\Big\{\int_{s}^{\infty}G(x)dx-\int_{-\infty}^{s}G(x)dx\Big\}=\int_{T}^{\infty}G(x)dx-\int_{-\infty}^{T}G(x)dx.

Consider
s\geq T
. Then,

\int_{T}^{\infty}G(x)dx-\int_{s}^{\infty}G(x)dx+\int_{-\infty}^{s}G(x)dx-\int_{-\infty}^{T}G(x)dx=\int_{T}^{s}G(x)dx+\int_{T}^{s}G(x)dx,

which is greater than or equal to zero. The case
s<T
follows similarly.
∎

The following result is stated as an exercise in
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
(cf. Problem 3.2.5). For completeness, we
decided to give the proof as well.

Lemma G.4
.

Let
(Z(s))_{s\in\mathbb{R}}
be a standard (two-sided) Brownian motion and let
a,b\in(0,\infty)
and
c\in\mathbb{R}
. Then,

\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\{aZ(s)+bs^{2}-cs\}=_{\mathcal{L}}\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)^{2/3}\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\{Z(s)+s^{2}\}+\frac{c}{2b}

By replacing
s
with
h(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(a/b)^{2/3}s+c/2b
, we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\{aZ(s)+bs^{2}-cs\}

\displaystyle=\operatorname*{argmin}_{h(s)\in\mathbb{R}}\{aZ(h(s))+bh(s)^{2}-ch(s)\}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)^{2/3}\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\{aZ(h(s))+bh(s)^{2}-ch(s)\}+\frac{c}{2b}.

Using the properties of Brownian motion, we have

aZ(h(s))=_{\mathcal{L}}a\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)^{1/3}Z(s)+aZ\Big(\frac{c}{2b}\Big)=_{\mathcal{L}}\frac{a^{4/3}}{b^{1/3}}Z(s)+aZ\Big(\frac{c}{2b}\Big)

and simple calculations yield

\displaystyle bh(s)^{2}-ch(s)

\displaystyle=b\Big(\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)^{4/3}s^{2}+\frac{c}{b}\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)^{2/3}s+\frac{c^{2}}{4b^{2}}\Big)-c\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)^{2/3}s-\frac{c^{2}}{2b}

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{4/3}}{b^{1/3}}s^{2}+c\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)^{2/3}s+\frac{c^{2}}{4b}-c\Big(\frac{a}{b}\Big)^{2/3}s-\frac{c^{2}}{2b}

\displaystyle=\frac{a^{4/3}}{b^{1/3}}s^{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{4b}-\frac{c^{2}}{2b}.

By a combination of these results,

\displaystyle\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\big\{aZ(h(s))+bh(s)^{2}-ch(s)\big\}

\displaystyle=_{\mathcal{L}}\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\Big\{\frac{a^{4/3}}{b^{1/3}}Z(s)+aZ\Big(\frac{c}{2b}\Big)+\frac{a^{4/3}}{b^{1/3}}s^{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{4b}-\frac{c^{2}}{2b}\Big\}

\displaystyle=_{\mathcal{L}}\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\Big\{\frac{a^{4/3}}{b^{1/3}}Z(s)+\frac{a^{4/3}}{b^{1/3}}s^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle=_{\mathcal{L}}\operatorname*{argmin}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}\big\{Z(s)+s^{2}\big\}

and the assertion follows.
∎

Lemma G.5
.

Let
\Phi_{n}
be defined as in Section
1.2
and
\Phi_{0}
continuous.
Then, for every
\varepsilon>0
and for every
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
, there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
, such that

|\Phi_{n}(y)-\Phi_{n}(x)|<\varepsilon\quad\text{for all }n>N.

For every
\varepsilon>0
, we know from continuity of
\Phi_{0}
in a neighborhood of zero that there exists
\delta>0
, such that

|\Phi_{0}(z)-\Phi_{0}(0)|<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\quad\text{for all }|z|<\delta.

Now for arbitrary
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
, choose
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that both,
|\delta_{n}y|<\delta
and
|\delta_{n}x|<\delta
for
all
n>N
. Then,

|\Phi_{n}(y)-\Phi_{n}(x)|=|\Phi_{0}(\delta_{n}y)-\Phi_{0}(\delta_{n}x)|\leq|\Phi_{0}(\delta_{n}y)-\Phi_{0}(0)|+|\Phi_{0}(0)-\Phi_{0}(\delta_{n}x)|<\varepsilon

and the assertion follows.
∎

We conclude this section with bounds on bracketing numbers for various function classes. Recall
\Phi_{n}(\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}})=\Phi_{0}(\delta_{n}\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}})
for some strictly increasing, continuous
\Phi_{0}:[-T,T]\rightarrow[0,1]
, where
\delta_{n}\searrow 0
denotes the level of feature impact.

Lemma G.6
.

For
\eta>0
and
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
with
-T\leq a<b\leq T
, let

\mathcal{G}_{n,\eta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\big\{g\colon[a,b]\to[0,1]\mid g=|f-\Phi_{n}|\text{ for }f\in\mathcal{F},\ \|g\|_{[a,b]}\leq\eta\big\}.

Then, there exist universal constants
L>0
and
C>0
, such that for any
\nu>0
,

N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{G}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{X}))\leq L^{C(\eta+\delta_{n})/\nu}.

Let
g=|f-\Phi_{n}|\in\mathcal{G}_{n,\eta}
and
[f_{L},f^{U}]
denote a
\nu
-bracket for
f\in\mathcal{F}
with respect to the

L^{2}(P_{X})
-distance, i.e.
f_{L}(x)\leq f(x)\leq f^{U}(x)
for all
x\in[a,b]
and
\|f^{U}-f_{L}\|_{L^{2}(P_{X})}\leq\nu
.
Let
K>0
denote a universal constant which may changes from line to line and note that

\displaystyle f(b)-f(a)

\displaystyle=f(b)-\Phi_{n}(b)+\Phi_{n}(b)-\Phi_{n}(a)+\Phi_{n}(a)-f(a)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|g\|_{[a,b]}+K\delta_{n}\leq K(\eta+\delta_{n}).

Then,

\displaystyle(f_{L}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{+}

\displaystyle\leq(f(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{+}\leq(f^{U}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{+},

\displaystyle(f^{U}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{-}

\displaystyle\leq(f(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{-}\leq(f_{L}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{-}

and

g(x)=(f(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{+}+(f(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{-}
, whence

\displaystyle g_{L}\colon[a,b]\to[0,1],\quad

\displaystyle g_{L}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(f_{L}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{+}+(f^{U}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{-}\ \text{ and}

\displaystyle g^{U}\colon[a,b]\to[0,1],\quad

\displaystyle g^{U}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(f_{L}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{-}+(f^{U}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{+}

satisfy
g_{L}(x)\leq g(x)\leq g^{U}(x)
for every
x\in[a,b]
.
Furthermore,

\displaystyle g^{U}(x)-g_{L}(x)

\displaystyle=(f_{L}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{-}+(f^{U}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{+}-\big((f_{L}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{+}+(f^{U}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{-}\big)

\displaystyle=(f^{U}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{+}-(f^{U}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{-}-\big((f_{L}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{+}-(f_{L}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))_{-}\big)

\displaystyle=f^{U}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x)-(f_{L}(x)-\Phi_{n}(x))

\displaystyle=f^{U}(x)-f_{L}(x)

and consequently, for
\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{f\in\mathcal{F}\mid f(b)-f(a)\leq K(\eta+\delta_{n})\}
,

N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{G}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{X}))\leq N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{F}_{n,\eta},L^{2}(P_{X}))=N_{[]}\Big(\frac{\nu}{K(\eta+\delta_{n})},\mathcal{F},L^{2}(P_{X})\Big).

By Theorem 2.7.9 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (2023)
, we obtain the existence of universal constants
L>0
and
C>0
, such that
the
L^{2}(P_{X})
-bracketing number of the class of monotone functions is bounded by

L^{C(\eta+\delta_{n})/\nu}
and the assertion follows.
∎

Lemma G.7
.

Let
S>0
.

(i)

Let
\mathcal{F}_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{f_{n,s,t}\mid s,t\in[-S,S]\}
, where

f_{n,s,t}\colon[-S,S]\times\{0,1\}\to\mathbb{R},\quad f_{n,s,t}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\big(\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}t\}}\big)

for
s,t\in[-S,S]
. Then, there exists a universal constant
K>0
, such that for any
\nu>0
,

N_{[]}\big(\nu,\mathcal{F}_{n},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\leq a_{n}^{2}\frac{K}{\nu^{4}}.

(ii)

For
j\in\mathbb{N}
, let

\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{f_{n,s}\mid s\in\mathbb{R},2^{j}<|s|^{\beta+1}\leq 2^{j+1}\}
, where

f_{n,s}\colon[-S,S]\times\{0,1\}\to\mathbb{R},\quad f_{n,s}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\big(\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}\}}\big)

for
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Then, there exists a universal constant
K>0
, such that for any
\nu>0
,

N_{[]}\big(\nu,\mathcal{F}_{n,j}^{\beta},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\leq a_{n}\frac{K}{\nu^{2}}2^{\frac{j+1}{\beta+1}}.

(iii)

Let
\mathcal{H}_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{h_{n,s,t}\mid s,t\in[0,1]\}
, where

h_{n,s,t}\colon[0,1]\times\{0,1\}\to\mathbb{R},\quad h_{n,s,t}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\big(\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq F_{X}^{-1}(s)\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq F_{X}^{-1}(t)\}}\big)

for
s,t\in[0,1]
. Then, there exists a universal constant
K>0
, such that for any
\nu>0
,

N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{H}_{n},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}}))\leq\frac{K}{\nu^{4}}.

(iv)

Let
\mathcal{H}_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{h_{n,s,t}\mid s,t\in[0,1]\}
, where

h_{n,s,t}\colon[0,1]\times\{0,1\}\to\mathbb{R},\quad h_{n,s,t}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))\big(\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq t\}}\big)

for
s,t\in[0,1]
. Then, there exists a universal constant
K>0
, such that for any
\nu>0
,

N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{H}_{n},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}}))\leq\frac{K}{\nu^{4}}.

Note that for deriving an upper bound on the bracketing number, we can omit the factor
(y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))
in the
definition of each function, as shown now exemplary for (i). For this, define

\mathcal{G}_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{g_{n,s,t}\mid s,t\in[-S,S]\}
,
where

g_{n,s,t}\colon[-S,S]\to\mathbb{R},\quad g_{n,s,t}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}t\}}.

Considering a function
f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
, there exists
g\in\mathcal{G}_{n}
, such that

f(x,y)=(y-\Phi_{n}(x))g(x)
. Now let
[g_{L},g^{U}]
denote a
\nu
-bracket for
g
in
L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})
, i.e. for
every
x\in[-S,S]
, we have
g_{L}(x)\leq g(x)\leq g^{U}(x)
and
\mathbb{E}[|g^{U}(X)-g_{L}(X)|^{2}]^{1/2}\leq\nu
. Defining

\displaystyle f_{L}\colon[-S,S]\times\{0,1\}\to\mathbb{R},\quad

\displaystyle f_{L}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-(1-y)\Phi_{n}(x_{0})g^{U}(x)+y(1-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))g_{L}(x)

\displaystyle f^{U}\colon[-S,S]\times\{0,1\}\to\mathbb{R},\quad

\displaystyle f^{U}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-(1-y)\Phi_{n}(x_{0})g_{L}(x)+y(1-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))g^{U}(x),

note that

\displaystyle f_{L}(x,0)=-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})g^{U}(x)

\displaystyle\leq-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})g(x)=f(x,0),

\displaystyle f_{L}(x,1)=(1-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))g_{L}(x)

\displaystyle\leq(1-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))g(x)=f(x,1)

and similarly,

\displaystyle f_{U}(x,0)=-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})g_{L}(x)

\displaystyle\geq-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})g(x)=f(x,0),

\displaystyle f_{U}(x,1)=(1-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))g^{U}(x)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))g(x)=f(x,1).

Further, we have

\displaystyle f^{U}(x,y)-f_{L}(x,y)

\displaystyle=-(1-y)\Phi_{n}(x_{0})g_{L}(x)+y(1-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))g^{U}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+(1-y)\Phi_{n}(x_{0})g^{U}(x)-y(1-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))g_{L}(x)

\displaystyle=(1-y)\Phi_{n}(x_{0})(g^{U}(x)-g_{L}(x))+y(1-\Phi_{n}(x_{0}))(g^{U}(x)-g_{L}(x))

\displaystyle=(g^{U}(x)-g_{L}(x))(\Phi_{n}(x_{0})-y\Phi_{n}(x_{0})+y-\Phi_{n}(x_{0})y)

\displaystyle=(g^{U}(x)-g_{L}(x))(\Phi_{n}(x_{0})-2y\Phi_{n}(x_{0})+y).

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|f^{U}(X,Y^{n})-f_{L}(X,Y^{n})|^{2}\big]^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[|(g^{U}(X)-g_{L}(X))(\Phi_{n}(x_{0})-2Y^{n}\Phi_{n}(x_{0})+Y^{n})|^{2}\big]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[|g^{U}(X)-g_{L}(X)|^{2}|\Phi_{n}(x_{0})-2Y^{n}\Phi_{n}(x_{0})+Y^{n}|^{2}\big]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[|g^{U}(X)-g_{L}(X)|^{2}\big]^{1/2}\leq\nu

and so we have

N_{[]}\big(\nu,\mathcal{F}_{n},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big)\leq N_{[]}\big(\nu,\mathcal{G}_{n},L^{2}(P_{\Phi_{n}})\big).

Analogously, this also follows for (ii), (iii) and (iv).

To construct the brackets for statement (i), note that by the previous result, it suffices to construct brackets for the function class
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{f_{n,s,t}|s,t\in[-S,S]\}
, where

f_{n,s,t}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}t\}}
for

x\in[-S,S]
. For this, let
\nu>0
, set
N(\nu)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{2Sa_{n}}{\nu^{2}}4\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}

and define for
i=1,\dots,\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor
,

s_{0}^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=-S,\qquad s_{i}^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=s_{i-1}^{n}+\frac{\nu^{2}}{4\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}a_{n}},\qquad s_{\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor+1}^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=S.

Then,
-S=s_{0}^{n}<s_{1}^{n}<\cdots<s_{\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor+1}^{n}=S
,

s_{i}^{n}-s_{i-1}^{n}\leq\frac{\nu^{2}}{4\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}a_{n}}
for
1\leq i\leq\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor+1
, and for
every
s,t\in[-S,S]
, there exists
i,j\in\{1,\dots,\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor+1\}
, such that

s_{i-1}^{n}\leq s\leq s_{i}^{n}
and
s_{j-1}^{n}\leq t\leq s_{j}^{n}
. Hence,

f_{n,s_{i-1}^{n},s_{j}^{n}}(x)\leq f_{n,s,t}(x)\leq f_{n,s_{i}^{n},s_{j-1}^{n}}(x)
for every
x\in\mathbb{R}
and

\displaystyle\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|f_{n,s_{i}^{n},s_{j-1}^{n}}(x)-f_{n,s_{i-1}^{n},s_{j}^{n}}(x)|^{2}dP_{X}(x)\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s_{i}^{n}\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s_{i-1}^{n}\}}+\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s_{j-1}^{n}\}}-\mathds{1}_{\{x\leq x_{0}+a_{n}s_{j}^{n}\}}|^{2}dP_{X}(x)\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq(a_{n}(s_{i}^{n}-s_{i-1}^{n})\|p_{X}\|_{\infty})^{1/2}+(a_{n}(s_{j}^{n}-s_{j-1}^{n})\|p_{X}\|_{\infty})^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Big(\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}a_{n}\frac{\nu^{2}}{4\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}a_{n}}\Big)^{1/2}=\nu,

whence
[f_{n,s_{i-1}^{n},s_{j}^{n}},f_{n,s_{i}^{n},s_{j-1}^{n}}]_{i,j=1,\dots,\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor+1}
define
\nu
-brackets for
\mathcal{G}_{n}
in
L^{2}(P_{X})
and

N_{[]}(\nu,\mathcal{G}_{n},L^{2}(P_{X}))\leq(\lfloor N(\nu)\rfloor+1)^{2}\leq\Big(1+\frac{2Sa_{n}}{\nu^{2}}4\|p_{X}\|_{\infty}\Big)^{2}.

Analogously, the brackets for the classes in (ii), (iii) and (iv) can be obtained.
∎
